A Diet Drinke For the springe tyme

Take of scuruie grasse halfe a pecke Wormewood one handfull
Rue halfe an handfull Brooklyme eight handfulls Liuerworte six
handfulls smallage foure handfulls peny royall halfe an handfull
Red Dock Leaues Rootes halfe a pounde nutmeggs halfe an ounce
Long pepper a quarter of an ounce, graines a quarter of an ounce
shred all these uery small make a pastie of it with Rye flower
two inches thick, bake it well, then slice it, three fingers
betwixt & put it into a Ielly Bagge, with a flinte stone and
soe hang it in a barrell of six shillings Beere and Lett it
be a fortnight before it be drawne

An Exelent purging dyet drink against the scurue Iandis or dropse
it opens the liuer splene & longues it clears the body bloud &

An aproued dyett drinck called the purging
ale to be taken euery spring & fall From the
first of Aprill to the end therof & from the

Take this alle as aboue sade will cleanse the body of all
Corrupt humours & wind it will purge & cleanse the
Stomacke liuer longes head & other parts of the body with
out ofence auoiding ther with much watter & wind if any

plextion of body & bloud makeing the countenance chearfull

be taken by an old body or child without ofence

23 bruesed ounces of sassafras wood 1/2 an ounce of rubarb let
all thes be seuerally brused into powder saueing the
sena which must be put in whole & when the alle
hath done working stope it up close leaueing good

in the morning take some warme broth an

To make 3 gallons of Dryett drinke

Guacum 4 oz Sasaphras wood of e roots 2 oz Salsa=
parilla 2 oz Harmadactulum 2 oz Polipodium 2 oz
Madder Roots 2 oz Red Docke roots 2 oz Cena 3 oz
Fennyll seed 2 oz Liquorish 2 oz Scurvigrasse one
handfull Scabius one handfull Agrimony one handfull
Nuttmeggs 2 oz Synamon one oz Large mace 2 oz
Aniseeds 2 oz Ginger 2 oz slice those t are to bee
sliced and bruise those t are to bee bruised then putt

Runlett of 4 Gallons hanging itt by strings to play
up and Downe then putt into itt 3 gallons of good
beare or Ayle when itt is ready to bee tunned and
havinge e bag with all e Ingredients hanginge still
afterward 3 dayes yw may drinke of itt att all times
as longe as long as itt lasteth takinge no other drinke

A Dyett Drinke to cleanse e blood

Take halfe a pecke of Scurvigrasse 2 penny worth
of Smallige one pennyworth of Lovige 2 penny
worth of Watercreases One penny worth of longe
peper One penny worth of Graines 2 penny worth
of Saffron stamp e hearbs and beatt e spices first
then putt all in a linnen bagge and lett them steepe

in e afternoone hange e linnen bagge in 3
gallons of good Ayle or beare with all e Ingredients
till all bee drunke up

The Lady Norriges Dyett drinke

Take of Cena 3 oz Yellow Docke roots Polipodium of e oake
of each one oz Choice Ruberbe and e best Agrige of

Toe make a powder to unstopp e liuer
Longues & milte

Take one ounce of Anniseede and one ounce of Licorish and one ounce of
Cummin seede and a quarter of an ounce of e Roote elicumpane, these dried
and made into a powder & drunken in possett drinke or ale or wine or
porrage, is good for e things aboue said.

+ The recepte of a drinke good against the fumes
from the spleane & to open the obstrucitons

Mrs Robenson Recept

Take of baume halfe an handfull as much tamarisk as
much louage boyle them in a pinte & halfe of alle poset
drinke a drought of it morning & euening for a week
together sweten itt as you pleas.

A medecine for e splen proued

Take melleat Cammamill of each 3 handfulls Roman Wormwid
planting dittiny of each a handfull beat m small in a stone
morter n put to 3 pound of fine rosen 12 ounces of bese wax
& a pound of shipes suet a pinte of white wine & melt all thes
together in an earthen pot soe leting them stand 7 dayes n let
m be perfictly strained & soe use m plaster wise.

For e Spleene a generall purgation

Take halfe an oz of Cena One dramme and a halfe of Ruberbe
cutt in slices halfe a dramme of fennill seeds bruised in a mor-
ter 20 grames of Synamon beaten grossely Halfe an oz
of Tameriske whole lett all these bee putt in a platter
or an Earthen pott then power uppon itt e third part of
a pinte of boylinge water and lett itt infuse so all
e night e vessell beinge covered In e morninge streyn
e infusion addinge to e expression one ounce of Sirrop of
Sciteran with Ruberbe then take e potion beinge fastinge
takinge a broth 2 houres after itt and keepe e chamber
a whole day itt will purge gently and sufficiently.

1st Take once a generall medicine

2ly Afterwards use e best dryed ripe Apples rosted in e morninge
beinge fastinge and betweene meals

3ly Once every month rub your side with this decoction of
tobacco 5 or 6 dayes.

Take one oz of St Christophers Tobacco and cutt itt in slices
and afterwards putt itt in an earthen pipkin with one pinte
of beare and lett itt boyle together till the beare be halfe
consumed and after streyn itt up for your use and use
e said decoction as aforesaid.

An Electurary for e same

Take of burag buglas suckery sage & rosmary flowers of e Conserve of each of
thes a quarter of on ounce of spear mint & Angelico of each half an ounce
mingle this all together n take 2 ounces of Conserve of marmelad of quinces & as
much of junipers n take an ounce of e powder of oris roots put to it 3 dropes of
e oyle of anyseeds as much of e oyle of nutmeges mingle all thes well together
put them to e Conserves ading 2 drames of fin powder of Rubarb as much
of Sena & as much Saferon beat all thes together in a marbell morter
put m into a gally pot & keep it for your use after they have drunk
e aforsaid dyet drinke let m take of this Electurary morning fasting
& at 4 of e Clock in e Afternoon & wn they goe to bed e bignes of a
walnut & so let m take it till it is spent observing e same dyet
they did wth e dyet drink.

Of the uertue of waters

Take water of buglass and ball called Melissa and e flowers of
Burrage, Reioyceth e hearte of man.

Water of e flowers of Elder is good for e hardnes of the melt
and it openeth e wayes of Urine and it flowreth e face

Water of of Rosemary is good for all cold passions of e sinewes

Water of plantane is good for bleeding, with refrigeration & stipticiti

Water of Sinke foile prouoketh Vrine and grauelye water

Water of Scabious is good for e passions of e brest as a cough & Canker

Water of e Rootes of bensens asperage fenell and parsly, and smallage
is good for e stone; for these herbs opens e vaines and puoketh vrine

Water of e herbe called gramen killeth wormes openeth
oppulations and puoketh Vrine

Water of night Shade or morraile is good for a hott Liuer and is uery
refrigeratiue

Water of maddell openeth e vaines of the matrice

Water of Cammemile flowers swageth inwarde paines

Water of mints comforteth a cold stomacke

Water of Bittanie openeth e vaines of the matrice

Water of Saxafrage Breaketh e stone in e blather and raines
and Drineth out grauely water

An excellent surrup for e Spleen

Take as many pared pipins stamped in a morter as will yield a pint
of surrup being strained n claryfyed over e fire & scume it n put
to it an ounce of Sena & a handfull  of sweet fenill seeds n let it
boyle away somewt about a quarter of a pint n strain it & put to it half a
pound of e best powder sugar 3 Spoonfulls of surrup of damask rosses &
this will give you about 12 stooles working gently ther must be 4 ounces of
surrup of rosses put into e pipen surrup while it is hot & to take 7 spoonfulls
at a tyme you may ade to a pound of this surrup an ounce of Ruhbarb
to e rest of e Ingredients

ffor e Spllen & Convouluisons

The decotion of e root Calamas drunk purgeth by urin helps e
pain in e side liver spleen convouluisons gripings & burstings it
helpeth pising by dropes

90

A Receipt for e Rickets

Take a potell of runninge water, a quarter of a pound of raisins
of e sunne stoned, 1 oz of Licoris scraped & sliced 1/2 of anyseeds 4 or
5 very good datts stoned 1 oz of harts horne, a good handfull of
Liuerwort well washed & picked; one handfull of Harts tounge
a handfull Lungworth as much ribworth a handfull of unset
hysop, a handfull of coltsfoote; 1/2 a handfull of maydenhaire
a few tops of penyreyall; Boyle all these together in e water
till it be halfe consumed boyled of these, then straine it
& sweeten it wth e best white suger candy, & give it e child
morninge & euening first & last & as often as you can get
e child to take it though but a spoonefull att a time
And take

+ An aprooued Drinke for e Ricketts

Take 3 pints of springe water one quart of white wine and
one handfull of sugar charvell halfe a hand full as much
vnsett hysop and balme putt them into e wine in a pipkin
close covered and lett it boyle gently vntill a pinte bee
consumed then putt in a quarter of a pound or lesse of
rasons of e sunne stoned a peice of Liquorice the length
of your finger scraped and sliced and then boyle itt
untill your reasons are pretty tender and soe keepe itt
for your use wth e hearbs in itt and lett e child drinke
of itt morninge noone & night and as oft more as e
child will drinke of itt

The ointment

Take of e roots of fox ferne halfe a pound of aylehoofe
Cammamell woormwood Red sage red fennill minte
Clivers brooke lime Scurvigrasse Rue fetherfew of each
a handfull boyle these in 6 pound of fresh buttur from
e Chirme & putt into e boylinge of itt halfe a pinte
of Muskidine & 3 or 4 flakes of large mace lett all
these boyle togeather lett all these boyle as longe
as yw thinke good then straine itt and keepe itt for
your use.

The Child must be anointed wth this night and morninge
the Chest & e Ribs and the Small of e backe, & e bendinge
of e knees & e hamms it must weare a doube cloth on
e stomach and soe round e wast it must not bee
changed in 3 weeks or a moneth.

But the drinke hath recouered many wthout e ointment.

+ For e Ricketts aprooued

Take of e inward barke of ash itt must bee of e younge
spriggs gathered in May and Tamerisse of each as much
as yw may hold betweene your four fingers and Thumme &
half as much of e inward barke of Ivy as of either of these
putt itt up into a quart bottell and fill your bottell wth 6s
beare & after it hath stood 3 or 4 dayes drinke thereoff
yw must fill 2 quart bottells at a time & when one is
out fill itt againe e child must drinke noe other
drinke.


91

+ A Note for the disease commonly
called e Rickets

Take Aniseeds Snymon white sugar candy of each 2 dramms a penny=
worth of Sanders Razons of e Sun one pound of Liuerwort one hand=
full Speare Mint one handfull Browne Sage & Hysop & Organe halfe
a handfull of each boyle these in a gallon of worth & putt itt in when
yw should putt in yo hops & lett it boyle as longe as yo hopps doe use to
come to a Pottle & give e child a small quantitye both at mor=
ninge & at night & make an ointment of all these hearbs boylinge
them in May buttur or other fresh buttur anointinge e child there=
with twice a day by e fire all over e flanks & belly.

+ A drinke for e Rickets

Take 4 gallons of beare wort & putt into itt 4 handfulls of Tamerisse
2 handfulls of greene barke of young ash one handfull of e barke
of Ivy 3 handfulls of Liuerwort one handfull of harts tongue a good
handfull of Razons of e sun stoned, & one of Currans, a Sticke or
two of Liquoras sliced boyle all these in e wort halfe an houre
& so tunne itt vp as yw do other beare wth all e hearbs in itt
lett this bee e childs ordinary drinke when itt is ten or twelve
dayes old.

+ For children t hath e Ricketts aprooued

Take halfe a pinte of Red Rose water & boyle therein 10 or 12
snayls & lett e child drinke itt sweeten itt wth syrropp of cloue
gilly flowers nott to sweete if e child hath not a feaver yw may
giue milke boyle wth snayls

For e Ricits

Take a pint of Spruce bear wch e Apothycarys sell, & give e Child a spoonful
or lese if you think it tow much in any drinke it takes or spoon meat in a morning
& befor a fire rub some of e same drinke into all its Ioints rubing it well in for
some tyme euery morning this hath helped & strengthened e Children inwardly
& outwardly.

To strengthen Childrens limbs t have e Recits

Take 2 or 3 whits of eges & stire m long till they come to an oyle
& rub e childs back & limbs & wrists wth it tis much comended

To sweeten e blood

Take 3 pints of watter & boyle it to a quart then put in a
pint of milke & boyle it & skime it & so drinke it insteed
of beer.

A drink to sweeten e blood good for itchings
or leperosie or kings euell by Do Lower

Take a pound of Dock roots, & as much dandelion & 3 handfulls
of harts toung & 3 of elder flowers half a pound of crud Antimony
brok into peices e docke roots must be dryed & sliced & e dandelion
dryed then put this into 4 galonds of small ale wn it is working
& as soon as it is clear drinke it, if you pleas you may put in
4 handfulls of ground Iuey, you must drinke no other drinke if it
be for a year or less as you like.


93

To make a Sirrop of Violett
or any other Floores

Take e Violett & pike them, from e whites, then weigh
them, take to an ounce of violett twelve times the weight
in Water, put e water into a cleane Pan set it on the fire
let it be almost at boyling and let the Violett be put into
an earthen Pott, when e water is hott put it to the violetts
& stirre them well together, and tye them close up with a
peece of leather next to e Pott and  paper above e leather
lett them stand by the fire in a constant heate two dayes
before you streine them, set them vpon Embers; whilst all
the coulo be out; when you have streined it take to a Gill
of this Iuce, 3 quart of a pound of loafe Sugar finely
beaten, to put into e Iuce, so stirre it & set it upon
Embers till it be thicke, take of the Scume, & so take
it off the fire and stirre it till it be cold; and then put
it in a Glasse and keepe for yor use.

To make surope of Reasons

Take your rasons & pres m or beate m & straine
m n putt your iuce into a glass violle & let it
stand 9 or 10 dayes & it will worke out all e drose
& your iuce will be cleare n you must power it out
carfulley through a strainer not sufering any of
e botom to goe in & to every pinte of e iuce
you must take 2 pound of sugar beate it very
smalle & putt it to your iuce n putt it upon e
fyer in a kettell of watter untill all your sugar
be melted & e scume rise you must not let it
boyle for if you doe it will Candy

To make a Sirrop of Violetts

Take 4 oz of Violetts e whits beinge cutt of putt them into
halfe a pinte of scaldinge runninge water and lett
them stand all night close covered strayned in e morning
and sett itt on a Chafin dish of coals and putt unto
itt 3 pound of fine loafe sugar by little and little
thoroughly melted then skimme itt cleane and putt itt
up the best way is to sun itt in e summer to dis=
solve the sugar

To make syrup of Violets

Take your violets ready picket and stamp them a little, then take faire water &
let it boyle a while then put e violets into a pot of Silver or Stone pipken wth
a cover and put soe much of that boyled water as will cover m & so let the flowres
infuse in e water being close covered till e couler is soaked out of the
leaves and they look white n straine them and take some what more Sugar
than the weight of the violets your sugar being finly beaten mix the sugar
& licqur together and put it into a pot of boyling water one houre and
let it be close couered then power your Surrup into a Skilit and let it
boyle 2 or 3 walmes then put it up for your vse.

Dr Baggs his Sirrop of Marsh
Mallows called Althea

Take of Marsh Mallow roots 2 oz of red Cicer one oz
of grasse roots of sparowgrasse roots of Liquorish and
razons of e sun streyne of each halfe an oz of e tops

A reiceipt to make Surrup of Cloue Gilliflowers

Thus you may make
surrop of roses

Take them and when they are picked wth e whites clipt from m take a pinte or
quart of faire water (though Burage) is better according to e quantity of surrup you
will make set it on e fire in a pipkin wth a cover or in a silver bason close
covered & when e water is ready to boyle then put the Gellyflowers to it as many as you
can thrust to be covered wth e water then let m lye and Infuse in the hot water
symbering on the fire close covered till they be growne white but e water must not boyle
then squese them cleane out of the licqur t all e streingth may got out into e
liqour then fill your liquor wth flowers again as before thus doe 3 tymes
that e licqour may be stronge then take soe much sugar as e lickour weight
set it on e fire & let it stand and symber till e sugar is dissolved let it boyle
2 or 3 waumes & stire it wth a spoon n take it of e fire & when it is allmost
cold put it up in your glase, if you let the surrup boyle too long it will loose its
couler

To make Surrupe of Violets or
Gilleflowers

The apotecarys way

Take a quarter of a pound of Clove gilleflowers or
Violets cut from e whites take watter & boyle it &
put it to e flowers in a silver cup or earthen pipken
t you may cover it put to the flowers a pint half a
pint of watter scalding hot & let it Infuse 24 howers
together then take to e licquour strained duble e
weight of very good powder sugar & put it into a Iugg
set in watter & let e watter boyle e Iuge being in it &
so let it be in e water till e sugar is dissoulved and
then put it up for your use.

To make Surrup of Rosses

Take e buds of damask roses & make it thick wth rosses
then put scalding watter on it & strain it & put in more
rosses into e licqur so doe for 9 dayes together wth
fresh rosses, & boyle it once weight of sugar


97

To make conserve of Red rosses

Take red rose budes & clipe off e yelowes & take 3 tymes e weight of hard sugar to once e
weight of e budes then beat e roses small in a morter & put in some of the sugar and so beat m
together & when it is almost beaten enough put in the full quantity of sugar to the roses & beat
them together till they be very small then put them up in a galley pot thrusting it very close together &
so keep it.

To make surrupe of Voyolots

Take your pickt vioulots being an ounce & put to m 2 ounces of scalding
hot watter & cover m close & let m stand 2 dayes so then strain m &
put to it 4 ounces of fine lofe sugar & set it in a iuge to boyle
in a pan as you doe for pudinges & so let it be in it till all e
sugar is dissoulved being close covered, then put it up for your use
being cold.

ffor to cuer e stone

Take poset drinke 3 nights together made wth white wine & boyle

Take 3 cloves of garlick goeing to bed 3 nights rowled up in butter or

A Medicen for e wind

Take a littell gett & dryed egg shell beaten to powder in a Spoonfull of
white wine, once a mounth

For e Colick

Take 3 cloves of garlike goeing to bed 3 nights together rowled up in

For e ston an Excelent medicine

Take posit drinke 3 nights together made wth white wine &

e stone called lapis Iudaicus as much as will lye upon
a groat or sixpence it must be beaten very fine & sersed

euery other day this has helpt thos so t they have never

for e Ston

Take a wine glase of Burdock leaves e symple watter of it euery

Another for e Ston

Take 4 or 5 spoonfulls of whit wine e iuce of a lemon & a spoonfull

for e wind

goeing to bed & in e morning in a glase of wine alle bear or tea or any
thinge you like best & so continue it a mounth together.

My Lady Duglows Receipt for e Stone Watter

Take a peck of hawes very clean pict from leaves & stalkes a
large handfull of Philopendula, & as much parsley, pound them
in a stone morter untill e stones are broken n put to m 2 quarts
of Saxifrage-watter. distilled when its iust on seeding if it be to
be had, & 2 quarts of e best whit wine. let it stand all night
in e morning put to m 2 ounces quarts of new milk & so
draw it off in a cold still with a moderat fire, mix e first
& last runing together & set it up for use

for e ston & colick

Swalow 2 or 3 peces of frech butter about e bigness of a nutmeg & drinke
a glase full of whit wine & fast till diner Probatum est

A powder for a woman that is licke to miscarey
by reasons of weaknes or frites or spraines or fludes

Take e stones of dattes dragons blude e Iaw bone of a picke
bolearmericke of each an ounce beat m all severally, of
red Corall on quarter of an ounce n waye of each equall
waight as aforsade n mix m together & give as much as
will lye upon a groate or sixepence in a mese of egge Caudel
or suga sopes or watter gruell for 3 or 4 mornings fasting
or longer if neede be probatum est

To preserue wemen from misCarigae

Take of dragons blude e waight of 2 pence of red Corall
powder a drame of ambergrease e waight of 2 barly
corines of East Indian Beser 3 barley Corins weaight
make all thes into very fine powder mingell m together
& keepe m in a close box & when you feare any
danger take as much of it at a tyme as will couer
a peney & rest quiet after it take it fasting in
some mace or broth alle or att night when you goe to bed or
att any tyme as often as you feare or suspect miscarige

Another for e same

thes tow
are my
Lord
Chester
fillds

Take  tredelles of 9 nuw layd eges as much Crimson
silk diyed in graine very smalle cutt, as will lye one
a 6 pence it must be shaued Sleauid silke tow sponfulls
of red rose watter e 3 parte of a good nutmige grated
a litell sugar beate thes all together take it when you
feelle your selfe not well & lye downe to rest if you cane
it must bee taken 3 tymes in e morninge it is best &
by gods helpe it will helpe you with out faille.

A Stringthing Elixtorey to be taken in any
Weakenes espechelley upon feare of miscareying

Take e consarue of e flowers of arckangells of Clarey of
each of these 2 ounces magistere of Corall white amber
in fine powder of each one drame & a halfe hartshorne
prepared owne drame with e surope of Corall or mertilles
make it in e forme of an Elextuerey you may take
goeing to bed e quantety of a small wallnut e like in
e morning.

To Bring Throwes on a woman in travell when she is Spent & hath
not strength to bring forth a dead Child or after birth if her throwes have left her
it will cause throwes to come againe

Take auents a handfull boyle it in a quantity of Ale wth a littel oatmeall thin & when
it is well boyled thicken it wth e yoalk of an Egg t it be thinn & give the woman
a good draught to drinke warme it will bring her throwes againe, & if the Child
be dead it will bring it away if it be in peices, & the after birth like wise its a
safe thinge sweeten e Caudell wth sugar to your tast

102

+ To prevent Abortion an Aproved  Plaister Called e Countes of Moulgraves

Take galengall one ounce, bole of Armenia, Sanguis draconis,  terra Sigillata, of each 30 graines, of mastick & of myrrh  of each a drame, make all these into finn powder wh halfe  an ounce of Ciprrus nutes beate into powder & worke it into  a past, wth Iuce of mirrhe, & mugwort, n ade halfe an ounce  of red sanders finly powdered, & wax as much as will sufice  to make soft e plaister

A Receipt to prevent miscarrying sent to  my Sister Cartwright by her neece e Duchesse of  Buckingham

Take e streames of 30 egges Spodium one ounce 24 leaves of gold,  mix all these together; give e patient one third part of this  medicine at one tyme fasting; fast about 3 howrs after, Take  e other 2 parts after e same manner, for 3 mornings together  wn you give this medicine inwardly apply a plaister to e back  of e patient, made of Emp: ad=hernium & Emp Mastick  of each a like quantity mixed together & to e belly of e  patient lay a plaister of Cypress Turpentin, upon e  nauell, e breath of e palm of your hand, lay upon e nauell  a little lint about e breath of 6d under e plaister, continue m 20 dayes & wn they grow dry renew them

This note under written send to e Apothecery.

To stop e fflowers in wemen wn they flood  or have m Immoderatly

Take a peice of any old blake silke & boyle it in vinagar and  apply it e part so affected as warme as they can bear it  this hath helped in half an howers tyme

An Exellent Receipt to stay ye flowers
in women if they come immodratly

Take e feete of an hare & burne them upon a Chafindish
of Charcoll & when they are well burned beat m to fine
powder n take a pinte of red wine burne it with dubell
refined sugar n take as much of e forsayd powder as will
lye upon a 6 pence & put it into a litell draught of e
wine & soe let e paitent drincke it morning & evening
for e space of 9 or 10 dayes & it helpth with gods blesing

For e staying off e flowers when they
immodarat often aproued

Take as much prepared Corall as will lye upon a tow pence
& as much bollearmerick finly beaten & searsed as will
lye upon a groat mixe it with a litell draught of red
wine mixt with Consarue of red roses & soe let e paitent
drinke it first & last & it helpeth spedeley you may
giue it to a woman nuwly dillevered & it stayeth e flowes

for e same

The red hollihok e flowers therof boyled in red wine
& drunke doth stay e ouer much flowing of m

ffor thos wch are apt to miscarry

spread suett upon a litle peice of whit leather & lay it on e
nauell & wear it continually spread a litle wax about e edges
to make it stick fast

To bring downe them when they stope in
young maides

Take 3 Gallons of Alewort put to it 3 good handfulls of white horehound
boyle it well in the wort when it is cold enough then put barme to it & let
e hirbe lye in it till it be barmed enough, & when you tun it up take out the
herbes out of the wort and let it be 10 dayes old before you drinke it drinke
a littell pot full in the morning and another at 4 a clock in the afternoon

Another for the same

Take a quart of Sack a handfull of Chery tre leaves ¼  of grose beaten
peper boyle these in e sack till one quarter be boyled away then power it
out into something to keep & take 3 spoonfulls in the morning fasting, 3
spoonfulls after diner & as much at night when you goe to bed cold when its
boyled one quarter away strain the leaves out of it very hard that no vertue
remaine behind & keep it close stopt in a bottell

ffor e palsie

The distilled watter of dafodillys doth cuer e palsie if e patient
be bathed & rubed wth e said liquor by e fire Probatum

An oyntmt for any old ach or numness in e Ioynts

Take 2 ounces of bores greas a quarter of a pint of brandy a quarter
of a pint of bulloks gall one handfull of Cammomile another of
e topes of red netles rosmary & wormwood a handfull shread e herbs
small put m into an Earthen pipken set it into an oven after bread
is drawn close e oven & allso let it be close covered & wn you take it

An excelent remedy for e palsie in e head

Take mustard made wth wine uinagar dry it in e oven wn it is throughly
dry beat it to powder take a quarter of an ounce of betony powder & mix
wth it half an ounce of whit sugar candy beaten into powder mix m alltogether
n take a spoonfull of it & mix it with bear & swalow it down & drinke a
draught after it & fast an hower after it doee like at 4 a cloke in e
afternoon & so doe it 9 dayes together & it will cuer it if cuerable

+ For e Dead Palsy aprooued or any benumdnesse
in the ioynts

Take as much as a Chessenutt of Metrydate euery night when yw
goe to bede

Take e stalkes of ould Rosemary halfe a pound cutt and shred them
take leaves of Bettany and sage dried of each 4 handfull two
shred nuttmeggs hange these with a flint stone or a peice of steele in
a Canvas bagge in a vessell of 3 gallons of ayle drinke no drinke
butt this all e winter drinke halfe a spoonefull or a spoonefull of
good Mustard in a little draft of this drinke euery morninge and
Eueninge anoint e Ridge of the backe on the same side that
his lame arme is on with this oyntment as followeth twice a day

Take Lauender flowers and bruised Rosemary of each one handfull
a quarter of a pinte of sallett oyle and 4 spoonefull of Aquavity
or any other stronge water boyle all these on the fire vntill e
Aquavity bee consumed then strayne itt out hard and when yw oynt
e backe take some of this oyle & mix itt wth Metridate and
Aquavity and soe apply itt wth oyntinge twice a day vntill yw
find amendment this hath by the blessinge of god recovered
some perfect well

A Receit for e Dead Palsie

Take oyle of Amber if itt be ine head droppe one droppe in e eare of
that side that is deade & one droppe into e side of e Nostrills & allso
lett there bee one droppe well rubbed one t side of e tongue &
lett t side of e face bee well rubbed wth e sayd oyle makinge
e chamber as hott as a stoue wth fire havinge a panne of fire
held close by while yw rubbe itt thus Rubbinge them 2 or 3
mornings together doth ordinarily recouer them butt yw must
droppe into e eare & Nostrell butt once the party must bee
kept in bed for 2 or 3 dayes together.

This is an aprooued Medicine t hath recouered many

A Receit for e Palsy

Take a pound of figs and slice them and boyle them in stronge ayl
to e thicknesse of a Pultise which apply warme to e place and
shift itt twice a day

For e Palsy in e tongue

Take beets and chafe them in your hand with Aquavit and lay
them blood warme to e nape of your necke and some under e root
of your tongue

Another for e same

Take new made Mustard and chop therein a quantity of Sage and
strike it uppon a linen cloth warme and lay to e nape of your necke

How to make a decocktion of Gyiacum

e Confection of liteficanus Almansoris Take on quarter of an

111

Walnutt water thus distilled is good against the
Plague or any infectious diseases by sickness

Take 2 pound of Walnutts 2 handfulls of Rue 2 pound of figges stamp m
in a morter then steepe them in as much sacke as will couer them for an
hour. still it altogether in a cold still put some fine sugar candye in the
glasse it drops into drinke as you see cause 3 spoonefulls or soe.

The Walnutt water called the water of life
for the vertues therof

Take walnutts in the beginninge of June beat m in a morter then still m in a stille
of leade and keepe them by it selfe then make a second water gather your walnutts
about midsumer or 2 or 3 days after use them as you did the other keepe e water
by it selfe alsoe then gather walnutts a forthnight after midsumer and use them
as you did the former; keepe the water by it selfe alsoe then take a quart of each
and put all together in a glasse still & keepe it close for your use.

The vertues of the simple Walnutt water

It will cure all manner of diseases and palsies beinge drunke wth wine fastinge
it is good for the eyes if you put one drop therin; it is to helpe the conception of
a woman if shee drinke therof moderatly one spoonefull in wine once a daye
it will make ones face faire if it be washt therwith it is good for infirmitye
within the body and draweth out all corruption if it be drunk with wine moderatelye
or with water it will kill wormes in the body drunke with or by it selfe who soe
drinketh it shall liue soe longe as nature may well continue lastly if you have
any wine that is turned put a litle violl full therof into it and stope it close, wthin
4 dayes it will recover it againe.

An excellent Cordiall water

Steep halfe a pound of hartshorne in 2 quarts of white wine put to it 2 good
handfulls of red sage an ounce of cinnamon let them all steepe 3 houres n still
them in a cold still put into the glasse it runs into a litle white sugar candy.
when you thinke it halfe runne out set other glasses under  keepe the first of
it selfe for that will be pretty stronge, this quantity must not be put into
the still all at once for it weakens it.

For a surfet

Take hyira pica one drame to a quart of e best whit anis seed watter
ade to it 2d of prepared cochenell brused put it in e botell of water
& shak it twice a day for 9 dayes together & let it stand in e sun or in
e chimney wher it may have a constant heat it helps surfets
good agt Infection take 3 spoonfulls fasting, it will give a stoll or
tow

112

The Lady Allens water. Good
for surfetts of meate Small
Pox feavers, or measels

Take sage, salladine, Rosemary, Rew, wormewood, Mugworth,
Pimpernell, Dragons, Scabious, Agrimony, Balme, Rosa solis
Scordium, Carduus Benedictus, Bettony Centaury, of euery one
of these hearbes a greate handfull, The Rootes Gentian wth
Tormentill, Angellico of each halfe an ounce, of Lignum vitae
one ounce, of Liquoris one ounce, the Rootes of Sidery halfe an
ounce. Slice the rootes and wash these hearbes, then shalke them
and dry them in a cleane cloth then shread them and put them
into a gallon of the best white wine, lett the Rootes and herbes
steepe altogether in it two dayes & two nightes then still it
in an ordinary still. Keepe the strongest by it selfe wch must
be taken, at the findeinge of any of any of these diseases
aboue written at night when the party is laid in bed, fower
spoonfull milke warme sweetened wth sugar , the same quantety
of the latter and weaker runninge will be best for children
the sooner it is prescribed and taken is better soe if that
night they lye a little warmer then usually they did, it will
cause a temperate sweate.

To make a good water

Dr Thoralds receiptes

Take Bettony, Balme and Carduus and Celondine of each two
handfull infuse these twenty fower houres in a potle of the best
white wine and still it, preserue this in quart Glasses close
stopt.

To make strawbery water

Take 3 or 4 pintes of strawberyes, bruse them wth your hand
and soe still them. Distill Resberyes in the like manner.

To make e Surfit watter of Red poppyes

Take as many Red popyes as you can well steep in 12 quarts of strong Ale,
put into it 4  of anyseeds 2  of Corriander seeds 2  of careyway seeds
rasions of e Sun Stoneyd & figes sliced of each half a pound 4  of  lickorish
slisd sliced your seeds brused let them Infuse 2 dayes & 2 nights then distill
them in an ordinary still.

+ To make stomach water. Lady Canarvans way

Take Cloves, Mace, Nutmegs Ginger of each a qter of an ounce Licoris Aniseeds
of each half an ounce cut & bruise all these take mint balm rosemary sage
Angelico Tamaris wormwood mugwort Carduus benedictus of each a little handfull
3 pints of whit wine 1 pint of aquavite pick & cutt the herbs put all these together
let them stand 24 houres then put them into a still in the morning and
still them with a soft fire the first bottle is the best the second wilbe the
smaller you must put into the Receiver half a pound of loaf sugar fine beaten

To make Chereis watter

A Receipt to make Balme watter

to make Saforon watter

Take tow quartes of stronge angellecoe watter & put halfe

this is good to chere e harte & much against mallencolley &
uery cordiale
you may make it stronger or weacker of e saforon as you ples

Doctor Denhams quitesons for surfets
& feauers

putt tow 3 pintes of e best aquauitea a drame of hierapicra
of e best & puerest yeallow cullere befor you put it in break
e powder with a cleane knife that ther be noe lumpes in it
let e glase be biger n will containe e stufe by one
quarter n stope it uery close with a corke that if you
turne it e botom upward noe drope may rune out n cover
it with paper & lether let it stand 8 dayes in e sune in
a tempereat warme place to digest shaking e glase very well ons
a day n let it stand 8 dayes more to setell befor you power it
from e botom or if you haue no present use it may stand as
longe as you will but be suer when you power it out let it be
soe slowly done as none of e botom be powered out with it &
soe put it up for your use

+ A watter to cause one to sweat

Take may flowers & distill m in a comon still & giue

To make the Cordiall powder

Take burnt hartshorne 2 drames white Corall 2 drames Coraline
2 drames prepared pearle one Scruple make m all into a fine
powder & take of it as much as will heeape upon a groat &
mixe it with as much of e powder of earthwormes as will
heape upon a groate & wett it in a spone with a litell posett
drinke made with hartshorne ivory marygold flowers &
drinke a draught of it after & use excersise fasting 2
howers after it.

A Recipt to make Spiritt of Clovgille flowers

Take halfe a pecke of Clove Gilleflowers cutt of e
white n thrust m close into a ston pott & put to
m a pottell of e best sake stope it very close &
tye it with a lether & set it in a seller 4 dayes
Then still it in an ordinary still take soe maney
gilflowers as befor soe cutt & putt m into a pott
e distilled watter & power upon e gilleflowers as you
did e sacke before & soe let it stand 3 or 4 dayes more
& n still it againe & power it upon halfe a peck
of freash gilleflowers more & soe doe this 3 or 4
tymes in e last stilling put 2 ounces of whit
sugar Candy finly beaten & put in to e glase that
receaveth it.

A good surfet watter

Take of mint & wormwood & Cardus an equall quantity
or if you will more of cardus & so much new milk as
will cover e hearbs & let it steep all night & distill it next
morning this is very much approved.

To make e same called milk watter

Take cardus mint angeleco wormwood rue e herb called medow sweet
balm thes herbs if dryed are better than to use them green of
each a like quantity & put as much new mi[l]ke as will cover
them & so still it in a pewter still this watter is accounted
good agst surfets & to sweeten e blood, allso to take a good
quantity of hogs lice swetens e blood.

To make e plague watter

Take of Sage Salendin rosemary Rew worwood Rosasollis mugwort
pimpernell dragon scabious Agerymony Balm Cordore Carus St John
wort Bittany flowers & leaves & of mary gold flowers & leaves of each of
thes 2 good handfulls then take e roots of tormentill Angelica & Elicompany
pinoy & licoris petecisus & buterbane fentia zedery of each
of thes an ounce let all thes hearbes be washed & shaken in a
lining bag till they be dry shreed m all together e roots being
thin sliced n put m into an Earthen pot wth a glaon & half
of whit wine so let it stand 2 dayes close covered stiring it
once a day destilling m in an ordinary rose still not a limbeck
still it wth a soft fire reserving of e first 3 pints of e 2d
a quart of e last a quart stop m Clos wth a Cork
you may give to a man or woman of e strongest 2 spoonfulls
though she be quick wth Child of e 2d sort twice as much
e Last sort given tow Children t are young a Spoonfull lukwarm
in a litle sugar or sugar candy

An Excelent receipt for e plague wch did help 600 in York & in one house
wher 8 were Infected 2 of m drunk of it & lived e other would not & dyed

Tak of ruh or hearbagrace a handfull half an handfull mary gold
fetherfew burnet sorell of each of thes as much, a good handfull of
e hearb Dragerion roots & wash all thes very well n bruse m &
put m into a pot of watter n boyle it well till it come to a quart let it cool
& strain it through a faire lining cloth n put in a peice of sugar & 3
drames of mitherdate this must be drunk befor e marks come forth

To make a good surfet watter of popeys Lady Winsors way

To 6 galands of brandy take 4 good bushells of popyes e feild ons t grow
in wheat gather m as much as posible in fair weather sift m clean from e
seeds peick pick m from e green knots as fast as they are sifted put m into e
brandy mixing m wth e other druges ready prepared allso 5 quarts of burage
& buglas flowers cowslipes & sag flowers & of rosmary flowers if you can get m
of each 3 pints a peck of gileflowers cliped 12 pound of blak cherys washed &
strained n break e stones in a morter & put m in allso a pound of figes a
pound of raisons half a pound of dates all stoned & cut an ounce of cardemon
seeds half an ounce of Anyseeds brused 2 good handfulls of Angeleco stalks slit
very small 6 ounces of liqurish scraped clean & infuse thes alltogether
for 14 dayes stiring it 2 or 3 tymes a day to e botom keep it close covered
n strain it from the druges swetening it wth a litle duble refined sugar
so botell it up for your use it will keep many yeares as good as at e first
when e watter is strained off all to e druges a galond more of whit wine
wth a good quantity of green balmn stilled in an ordinary still this
makes a very good cordiall watter & you may ade some more blak cherys

118 + To make a good surfet watter of popeys Mrs Purfrys
Take 2 quarts of brandy sack or aquauity one pound of lose sugar
set it in e sun 20 dayes shaking it together each day as you se
fit set it out for 2 or 3 howers in e heat of e day puting in 60
cloves as much synamond & a half peny worth of scotchenell hang
all this in a bag in a glasse to sun then put into a pot e aquavity
so suned half a bushell of red popeys of e feild to steep together for
24 howers then strain it out for your use keping it close stopt about
a spoonfull at a tyme is enough to take it is uery good for any distemper
as small pox or measells but singular good for surfets you may make
a watter wth ye popyes & spice stilled wth good herbs & aquiuita or wine
or wt you will.

The Marquess of Dorchesters Cordeall water vsed by the
Family of e Pierponts.

R Dragons, Rosemary, Wormwood, sage, Scordium, Mugwort, Scabius Balm, Carduus
Tormentill roots & leaues, tops of Betony flowers & leaues pimpernill Add woodsorrill Egrimony
Rue of erdy one of these half a pound Liquerish 4 ounces Elicampane 1 ounce, shred the herbs &
slice the rootes then put them into 3 Gallons of e best whit wine, let them stand couered
2 days & 2 nights stirring them morning & Euening, then take some of the herbs out of
the wine lightly squeezed with yor hand & fill yor still in the morning, an ordinary still &
let it be stilling 12 hours with a reasonable quick fire, then take the rest of the herbs
& wine & still them in a Limbeck as you do other strong waters till all the strength of the
herbs & wine be gone, you may make 3 sorts of water in yor Limbeck & 2 of your still,
put the strongest sort of water you still in yo ordinary Still to the smalest you still in yor Limbeck
& so you may keep it all from Musting or frost 3 or 4 years, keep some of your 3 sorts to
delay the heat of the other, when you giue it little Children vnder 4 age of age put some
Sugar to it or syrop of Gillifloures, let it be almost bloud warm giving a quantity as you
think sutable to the disease: 9 or 10 spoonfulls is enough for man or woman.

This Cordiall is good against burning heats, plague, small pox, measells, any infectious
Disease from the heart after a surfet, good to be given to Children in fitts of an Ague
when they take it lye down & sweat & fast 2 hours before & after it.

Receipts for Ciurgerey

A soueran oyle for to drope into wounds hurt with uermin weapons or pines pricked
Take e leaues flowers & seeds of St Iohns worte 2 great handfulls
of e leaves & flowers of balsom & valerean of each one handfull
bruse m & put m into a great dubell glase ade therto a quart of
white wine 4 poundes of oyle of ollive 2 poundes of oyle turpintin
set e glase stoped in e sunne 8 or 1? 10 dayes n bowle it in e
glase in a kittell of watter with some straw att e botome
which donne strayne e licqor from e hearbes & doe as you
did before putting in ye licke quantety of hearbes & doe as
you did befor putting ye licke q flowers & seeds & boyle it againe
n straine it & keepe it for your use.

Mr Moullers fomentation for an extreame ach
or lamness
Take 6 galons of runing watter set it one in a brase pote
or skillet itt must be alle measuer put into it 3 alle quartes
of wood ashes n put of wormwood senterey & sant Iohns wort
of each a great bunch let thes boyle a bout a gallon away
ade a handfull of white sallt soe straine it & put it
into bottells & keepe it for your use stope it close this must
be used exceding hote with nuw white coten acording to
e bignes of e place greiued it is uery Comfortable & uery
streingthing if you will you may ade muskadine in e useing
of it but that must not boyle with it.

A green oyle for a bruse

Take a galon of sallet oyle put it into an earthen pote n
take sutherenwood sage Cammamile worme wood weigh m &
take a licke quantety of each shread m & put m into
e oyle to steepe 12 or 214 howers n take reed rose budes
e quantety of halfe a peccke cutt of e whites of m & putt
m into ye oyle 4 handfulls of lauender topes picked
stiring m every day n putt m into a brase panne & boyle
it upon a soft fyer e space of 2 howers & stire it all e
whille when it hath boyled an hower take it of & putt
into it halfe a pinte of aquaita n set it one & let it boyle
one halfe hower n take it of againe & put into it halfe
a pinte more of aquauita n set it one e fyer & let it
boyle half an hower after that strayne it through a
Clean cloth into a bason & soe let it stand till it be cold
n putt it into a glase & keepe it e hearbes must be
gathered in May & layde in steepe till you cane haue the
rose budes & lauender you must put e hearbes into e oyle
when it is boyling or else it will not be greane.

An Exelent receipte for a bruse all though it be neuer soe bad in any parte of ye limes

Take cow dounge & sprinkell it with a litell wine uinagar &
putt into it a litell barowes grease & warme it in a frying pane
& applye it as hote as e paitent cane induer it & giue him
to drinke permecitey or irish slate or moma or some of e
ballsom & keepe him warme & this by gods helpe will
Cuer him in a shorte tyme.

To make e Paraceltion plaster

Take of these 4 gumes that is to say gallbanado opopany of each an ounce
Amoniagum & bedellium of each 2 ounces let m be beaten uery small
put m into an earthen potte that will hold 3 pintes it must be well
leaded or glased n power upon e sade gumes a pinte of e best wine
uinagar & couer m close that nothing fall into m & let m remain
a day & a night n take a quarter of a pinte more of uinagar &
putt into m stiring m a litell n sett m one e fyer untill
they be throughly mellted contually stiring m n take of
nuw canuas that is uery thine & straine m into e potte againe
& soe sett m upon a uery gentell fyer of coalles keeping e fyer from
e sides of e potte that e gumes may simper untill e uinagar be
all soden away & euaporated simper e gumes untill they will spread
licke a salue in e simpering of it you must continalley stire
it with out seaseing let e gumes be browne & take heede lest any
thinge fall into m n take a quart of e best oyle oliues &
putt it into a pott well leaded or glased that holdeth a pottell
or more & set it one a soft fyer of coalles & put into it halfe
a pound of nuw wax cutt it into small peces & soe let it melt
in e oyle by leaseuer & when you are suer e wax is throughly
melted put into it a pound & a halfe of lithrage of gold beaten
into uery fine powder breake e powder with a Cleane knife
that ther be no lumpes stiring m continalley with a stick
or spateuer or such like by litell untill e nend thinges be well
mellted together & n it will be a tauney Coller & thicke n take
it of e fyer when it hath done simppering a litell while n take
of e fore sayd gumes that wer first boyled & with your stick or
spateuer take of m e quantety of a nutt & putt e same into
e same matter stiring it continauley & soe by litell at seuerall
tymes putt in your gumes by such a licke quantety untill e sayd
gimes be all putt in & be well mixed together with e other
things your gumes will be allmost an hower aputting in therfor
when you se it begin to be Cold set it one a few Imbers taking
great heed it be not ouer hotte for then it will rune ouer into e
fyer for it is uery hotte of it selfe but if e worst should
hapen that it sudenly begine to rune into e fyer that you
cannot stay it haue a bowle of watter by you & quickly
put it into e watter & set it one e fyer againe when it
is taken out of e & when ye gumes are well putt in &
throughly mixed & melted after put in e thinges following take
of e 2 kindes of Aristologiah Rotundo Calaminaris Myrthe
Friankencenes of each of m one ounce beate m into uery fine
powder & searese it through a uery fine cloth twice power in e
myrth after e powder & Frankensence last of all mingell m in a
paper & power m into e same matter leasuerly then power into
it an ounce of e oyle of bayse & lastly putt therin pure turpentin
4 ounces when you put in your turpintine take it of e fyer a litell
while & stirre it with uery great dilegence else your turpintin
will cause it to runne into e fyer & if you would know wither
it be well & suffently soden putt a litell into Cold watter & if
it be not soe soft that it will cleaue to your finger but if
it clave to your finger it is tow softe & thefor let it simper
longer untill it be uery well simpered n take it of ye fyer & 60
putt soe much of it upon a bord anoyented with oyle of roses as
will make a great rowle & when it is cold you may handell it

anoynt your hands with oyle of Cammimill or oyle of roses
& knead it well with your hands & soe make it up into a

The Virtues of this plaster followeth

it is good for old & nuw sores it dryeth it Cleanseth it
breedeth good flesh it confermeth comforteth & healeth
more in one weeke then any other in 8 weekes it will not
sufer any sore to puterefey or curopt or any dead flesh to
grow. Also for sinewes brused or cutt or pricked with
a thorne or other wise hurte it is exelent it drawes
out of wounds wood or iron or lead & any other thinges it
cuereth biteings of uenemous beast it cane search all
kind of boyles & impostumes to ripen & most Exelent
against a Canker a thistolo e shingelles or St anthons
fire allso a present helpe to asswage all paines & aches &
for all kind of woundes allso it is found a present helpe
for bones out of Iont by laying 2 or 3 plasters at the most
it helpes in 4 dayes armed out of Ionte perfectly soe
that the partey feeles noe paine nor acth afterward you
must not tentt m except they matter befor they
come to m or 2 plasters is sufenet to healle it or any
other sore or swellings burst dead fleash it will not sufer
any ille to bred.

+ An Exelent salve to cuer any

Take a pinte of e best salet oyle 2 peney worth of red lead

+ for a bruse

Take e gall of an oxe ognions & fry m with a quantty
of sheeps suette & with a fine lining cloth apply it to

A Receipt for a uiolent ache that is
come with a heate & Could

Take a good handfull of grene sage & as much of Cammamile
& as much of rose marey topes & boyle all thes together in
a pinte of aquauita upon a Chafendish of coales in a puter
dish you must let boyle till e hearb be tender & e aquati
consumed & when it is all most consumed putt in a quarter
of a pinte of neatsfoote oyle & n let ye partey greiued
be fomented ther with & tye e hearbes uery thicke round
a bout it & let it be aplyed twise a day to e place till
it be well.

A watter to Cleanse an owld sore of
e dead flesh & to hele the sore

Taka a quarte of smiths watter & aquarte of plantine
watter & an ounce of mercuery putt all thes together & let
m boyle a prettey whille n take it of e fyer & let it stand
& Coulle n take a browne paper & put it upon a funerll & soe
let it drope by litell through your paper into your bottell
it will be 4 or 5 dayes a droping when you use it you must
warme a littell & dipe linte & soe apply it to e soare

A wound drinke

Take southeren wood wormewood buglice mugworte ribworte
wood betenoy sanacle plantine dandilion white bottells brambell
budes egeremony wild angellecow Comferey hawthorne budes mint
scabous strawberey leaues dase roots housleck auens uilot
leaues oaken leaues Cinckfolle of each one handfull ade to thes
a pottell of white wine & a gallen of runing watter boyle
m all together till on halfe be consumed of the licqor n strain
it from e hearbes & putt into it a quarte of honey & soe
boyle it againe resarve it in a glase Close stoped for your use
let the paitent drinke 6 sponfulls at a tyme in e morning
fasting & soe last at night contieneue e takeing of it soe
longe as you see ocation
Thus cuers old sores wemens breast puterefeyed bones causing m
to scale it is good for aches in e stomacke breakes & impostum
stanches blud broken in body & causeth bloud to fall out of e
body wounded you must not giue it to cuer a brest befor it be
broke you must putt noe tent in it but aply a searcloth

A drinke to cuer an old soar

Take leaues of ualleren red sage betoney a quarter of a pinte
of hempseed red Cabbage red brambles of uilots of plantine
sinckfold sanicle strawberes doues fote woodbine Egeremoney
Garden mader rootts Comfery roottes hasell leaues of every one
a handfull kernells of hasel nutts halfe a pinte bruse all thes
together n boyle m gently in a quarter or more of white wine
& a good pinte of stronge bere till a pinte be consumed n
straine it & afterwardes boyle it againe softly ading ther
to halfe a pinte of honey let it boyle as longe as it yeldes
any scume n take it of drinke of this warme tow or 3 sponfulls
first & last & apply to this sore a litell linte diped in e
liqor

+ A Sercloth for a spraine
Take wax & dere suett of each quantety alicke soe much rosen
as one of thes melte m together in a Skilet n deep a pece
of nuw lockorm as bige as e sore & when it is allmost cold
wrape it about e place greived

+ A plaster for e paine in e Stomake
Take wormwod minte & Callamint sage & uinagar & stampe
thes & tost white bread till it be browne & put e evu vries
ther of crumed small to it & make it all together like a plester
& lay it to e stomake

Exelent Venetion Signior Lucattella

To draw out a Thorne

Take red or white wine & wheaten flower & temper m
together n take a pretty quantety of e  middel barke of a thorne
trey & bruse it & put to e aforesade n take as much
blacke sope as an wallnutt & putt therto & boyle m
alle together till they be thicke n make a plaster
therof & lay it hott as you can suffer it unto e place
wher e thorne is & it will draw it out

+ A Soueran Medeceine for one biten with
a Made doge or Stung with an adder

a quantety of Garlick a quantety of Ruw a quantety
of salt & as much hoges grease as of any other of e
ingredents beat m all very well together & apply m
to e place soe bitten

To helpe a bruse or spraine

Take a good handfull of Cammamill boyle it in your
own watter till it be soft n lay it upon e place
greiued as hott as you cane sufer it

ffor an ach or paine in e bake or bones

Take as much blake stone pich as a wallnutt & 2 peney
worth of narve oyle boyle m together in an earthen
pott boyle it a pretty while & scume it very cleane then
straine it into another pott n putt in 2 ounces of

thes haue a warme or tow more att e fyer & rub
e place greiued with this. this is uery good against

it still though it be a fortnight it is e better but
within 3 dayes tymes anoynting you shall have ease & be
cuered

This hath Cuered some that were Brused t went 3 years with Stillts

A Receipt for any aches or
Burnings or Scalldings

Take a new pipkin putt therin a pinte of good Salet oyle
n put therto a quarter of a pound of bes wax 2 ounces
of washed Turpentine 5 or 7 sponfulls of white wine
as much reed rose watter n boyle m with a soft
fyer as it begins to boyle you must keepe it stiring
Sometymes drope a litell one a Cleane trencher or
a substance n take it of the fyer being allmost Cold
beaton & searsed you must keep it stiring tull all e
Sanders be in.

A Purge for e Kings evill
Take an ounce of cara castina divide it in 3 part giue e child 1/3 part dissolued in warm whit wine
for 3 days together about new moon & full let him be orderd in purging Phisick if it work not increase
the quantity.

Sr Kenelin Digbies Diet Ale for a Fistula or Dropsy

Tale 4 gallons of Ale 400 woodliss or souepalins a little hanfull of sage as much Rue beat e Souep
& herbs together & in a bag put it inthe Ale when it has done working let it be yor ordnary drinke.

La Huibman.  A Diet Drink for Kingsevill or any cold rewnes

Take Liverwort hurtstonge scuruy grass Agrimony Mugwort & Brooklins of each like quantity halfe
a peck in all pick & wash them & tun them in 3 gallons of ordnry beere or Ale put 12d of Lignum vite
& let the party drink it constantly it wilbe ready to drink in 8 dayes.

+ For the pallat of the Mouth
Take a peece of Alum & yolk of an Egg out in a silver porniger Stirred together over e fire till
it be so hard that will beat to powder put of this pouder frequent into yor mouth.

+ for to knit a rib t is broken or any inward bruse

Take a good quantity of balm bruse it & boyle it in poset drink
strain it & give it e party to drinke

good for a bruse

Take a sheepes head & boyle it in water till it comes all to peices, & boyle
in it a good handfull of sage a handfull of rosmary a handfull of Centary a
handfull of St Iohns wort & wn it is enough skime it & strain, it & then dip in
your flanill swaths & rowle m up & put them on as hott as can be enduerd
& wn e one is on e other must heat, this must be done at least half an
hower twice or thrice in a day & continued about 10 dayes or more

+ To make oyle of mace

To a pint of salet oyle take a handfull of mace put it into a glas
& stope it close & boyle it so stoped e space of 6 howers in a pot of
watter & so set it in e sun a mounth in e hotest tyme of e year

ffor ye Kings Evell

Take a handfull of kings-wort otherwise called Senacle topes of
brome as much betony & coltsfoot liverwort & smalge  costmary
handfulls of each boyle all this in a Galond of Spring watter
in a pipkin close couered for 2 howers gently wn it is cold
strain it & put it into bottles drink of it twice a day a quarter
of a pinte fasting after it this is to be drink 6 weekes spring
& fall this receipt has cuored some of e distemper, it is good
to take a purge after it, & if any sores take of e plaister &
lay on only an oaken leafe euery day wch will dry up e sore

Lady Husseys yalow salve
The yalow salve good to draw a Thorn out of e
flesh or any festered sore t needeth to be drawn

Take a quantity of Venus Turpintine as much Bees wax
all most as much Rosin a little bit of fresh Butter out of
e Churn unsalted, Boyle all thes together in a Skilit
& as it boyles it will rise up to e top of e Skillit and
then it will fall of it self again then take it off,
it is boyled enough then pour it forth into oyster shells or
work it into a roule wn cold enough to touch & so keep
it for your use either way

To make oyle of Almonds or Nutts

Take them or either of them as yu please & blanch them & putt them
into a pott full full of holes in e bottom & sett itt ouer a pott
of seethinge water & streyne forth e oyle & vse

To make oyle Almonds Wallnutts & Small nutts

Take them and bray them as small as possible then putt them
into a hard presse & sett a vessell under to keepe your oyle
this oyle is very precious yw must itt into a glasse lett your
Nutts be cracked ere yw be att the stuffe putt them into a linnen
cloth ere yw presse them

Oyle of Roses

Take 3 pints of faire water & putt itt into a new Earthen
pipkin & heatt itt scaldinge hott then stuffe itt full of Damask
Roses picked from e stalkes & lett itt stand on e Embers
4 or 5 houres then powre them out into a Cullender into a deepe
bason & presse ye Roses hard then heatt againet Liquer as
before & fill itt wth Roses & soe do itt 4 or 5 times and when yw
haue hard streyned e last infusion putt ye Liquor wch may bee
allmost a quart more or lesse into a glasse wth so wide a Mouth
as yw may putt in your hand into itt and lett itt hold 3 quarts
or a gallon & putt to itt a pottle of good sallett oyle & couer itt with
e parchment close & sunne itt for 4 or 5 dayes & then putt
into itt a greatt quantity of redde Rose budds beinge cutt from e
whites & searsed and soe sunne itt as longe as e sunne is of
force this is good for blastings of e face dippinge a cloth into
itt & lay itt thereon for all in flamacons beinge anointed with
e hand

To make e oyle of Snails

Take Valerian woodbine leaves Deuiles bitt Adders tongue night
shade Hartstongue Houndstongue Serpents tongue Capon feather
St Iohns wort Houslick of each a handfull then take a quart
of Snails wth their shells breake them in a wooden bowle wth e
Hearbs lett ye Hearbs be chopt before you putt them to e snaills
then take all most halfe an oz of Allum beaten small & 2 oz of
wax & 2 oz of Honney putt these in e pott yw will keepe itt in
lett your Hearbs boyle vntill they are crisppe as if they were
fryed in a panne then streyne itt to youre things in e pott
Tis good for Aches Swellings wounds Shrunke synews tis
good allso for ye goute comminge of a hott cause if e swellinge
bee greatt & Redde take oatmeale beaten &boyle itt in faire
water very thicke and as yw vse itt putt in some of this ointment
& lay itt on e Sore.

To take e fire out of a scald or burne

Take some onyons & pille e shell of & chope m small & aply to e
place as soon as it is done & this will take e fire out and has ben
aproved

To tak fire out of a burn

Tak a roten aple & lay to it & it will fetch out e fire & heale
it being still fresh aplyed to it, also if you rub e burn wth
your Spitle, or wth wt you can blow out of your nose wch is
better
another bath it in milke & it will get e fire out
another rub or lay salt on it & it will get out e fire
another for a scald doe it wth brandy

To cuer a burn spedly or scald

Take some smal oatmeal & boyle it wth new milk & boyle in it
some malows & uoylet leaves & some cooling pultice herbs & put
in some muton suet & apply it to it & so doe it morning and
Euening wth fresh pultice 3 or 4 dayes binding e pultice well
well to it then take some salve called basilicon wch you may
have at e Apothecarys & lay a plaister all over it & take it off
once a day & as you se it heals only wipe e plaister not puting
on fresh

Another

Take salet oyle or linseed oyl & to a pint of oyle of A
penith of red lead & boyle it to e thickness of a plaister &
then use e salve to it & it will cuer dresing it once or twice
a day at e first

To make a pultice to get fire out of a scald
or burn

boyle white bread & watter together & put in a litle bors greas & let
it lye on one night & as soon as you can anoynt it wth wt you please

To good e fire out of a burn

Take mude out of a sinck or high way & clape it one
immediatly & in an howers tyme renew it again once or
twice & this will take e fire out & if e blisters be not
broke they will desg dry away

Another

Take e inward bark of green Elme & put it into a litle
Spring watter litle more then to wet it squez e bark out
& anoynt e burn wth t water wch will be a Ielly & so
lay it on & it will take e fire out, then lay some healing
salve or oynmt wch may be made wth e inner bark of Elder & cream
boyled together

131

How to prepare worms for powder

Gather your worms att night then putt them into an earthen
pott with ffennell to purge them in e morninge take them out
and putt them into a platter and bast a handfull of bay salt
amongst them then beatt them and e salt together a good while
with your hand and mash them in severall waters till e
their slimes be goonne then cutt off a little of each of e ends of
them unrip them cleare thorow and scrape out all e earth
with in them and putt them into faire water till your hand well cleaned them all
Then shift them into clean waters washinge them till
yw haue well cleaned them all
Then shift them into clean waters washinge them till e
water bee very clear lastly wash them in white wine
which whill raise a greatt frothinesse
Take them from t and putt a little fresh white wine to them and
lett them stand all night in itt in e morninge take them and dry
them in a cloth and sett them in an Ouen t is not very hott laying
them flatt in an earthen platter or about a siue
You must be sure the Ouen be not too hott for if they dry too hasti-
ly they will bee spoild when they are thus dryed beatt them to powder
they are very hard to beatt butt with pains they will bee very fine
powder You must putt to 2 oz of this powder of worms halfe
an oz of Calsined iuory halfe an oz of Callsined Hartshorne
This powder mingled together yw must take euery morninge
for 6 weeks together as much of itt as e weight of 8d and
with itt and after itt e possett drinke followinge

An openinge possett drinke to take with e powder

Take 2 quarts of clear possett drinke then take of Sparrowgrasse
roots and of ffenill root of Dandelyon and Parsly and of each a root
of Scursonora root otherwise calld vipergrasse 2 Pippins or Pearmains
sliced a handfull of razons of e sun stoned halfe a handfull of
Agrimony as much Scabius when yw haue made youer roots ready to
putt in yw must stamp them a little then putt all the things to-
gether and lett them boyle in e possett drinke till halfe bee
consumed and lett it stand in a Pipkin with these things all e
while yw drinke itt yw must make this possett drinke thrice a ^weeke
When yw be take your powder yw must itt in a spoone and only wett
itt with some of this possett drinke and so swallow itt downe then
wash itt downe againe with 2 or 3 spoon fulls of itt warme and
halfe an houre after itt drinke 1/2 a pinte of itt warme and eat
nothinge else till dinner t day

The purge to be taken once a weeke all e while yw take
e powder followinge

Two oz of sirrop of Succory with double infusion of Ruberb putt
into itt a little of e openinge possett Ayle
The day yw take e purge yw must not take e worm powder but keepe your
chamber
Take this purge e day before yw take e worm powder

A  Dyett drinke for faintnesse or ye scurvy
Take halfe a pound of Sasaparilla Hartshorne an  Ivory of each a quarter of a pound a quarter of a pound of yellow & white sanders yt is of each 2 oz Marsh Mallow roots Centinian roots ferringe roots beinge all dryed of each one oz and 1/2 flowers of Rosemary Bettany Sage & Srikundus of each a hand full make all these in a ppowder and putt itt into a bagge and hange itt in a vessell with ye weight and … itt up with 2 gallons of good Ayle then lett itt stand up close 4 or 5 dayes and drinke a good draught morninge and eveninge if you feare ye Scurvy putt in a good handfull of yt grasse with powders

An aproved good dyett drinke to be taken in ye Springe or fall
Jan sure
Take ye root of Marke Ruberbe 4 oz ferne root 2 oz Succory root Burnett root Fennill root of each one oz ye herbs of Scurvy-grasse Surcory Hartstongue Geronander Hemitary Brookelime Watercreases Seabins Agrimony of each one handfull 2 sliced pipeons with ye parings ye flowers of primroses violetts broome buds of each halfe a handfull ivory scrape one oz and 1/2 3 spoone fulls of pialet capers dryed up in a napkin 3 oz of Cena one oz and a halfe of very good Ruberb Fennill seed Corimeggs cutt in peices putt up all in a bag and yt bag in 5 or 4 gallons of new beare or ayle or which you may take 3 or 4 dayes after itt hath beene putt up

An Excelent dyet drink to be given wth scurvygrasse agt ye dropsy & scuvye
Take Gyacaum 4 ounces sasaparilla 6 ounces China one ounce liqurice 2 ounces rasions of ye sun 2 ounces Anesseeds half an ouce steep thes together in 6 quartes of spring watter 12 howers then boyle this tow 2 quarts yn put in Agremony maidenhair betony of each a handfull boyle ym moderatly wth a soft fire yn set ym from ye fire let ym setle yn let it run 3 or 4 tymes through an Hypocras bag let it be very Clear yn take of this duet drinke a pint & an halfe at a tyme & set on ye fire to boyle yn put in sudenly half a pint of ye Juce of Scuvygrasse & it will presert cast a grat scum like a poset and take of ye curd clear & drinke of this morning & evening 6 ounces at a tyme warme & make more as ye other is drunke ye Juce of Scurygrass must be made nuw

An aproved remedy for a scald head
Take to a pound of May butter vnsalted a quart of the strongest Ayle
you can get & boyle it longe together till itt lookes wery black and
cut of the haire and anoynt itt all over where the scald is twice a day
& this has been approued to be a certine cuer if you put in a handfull of
wormewood shread & boyled wth it twill cuer sooner my Lady Husseys often approved

To make haire grow wch comes of wth any accedeint of Sicknesse
+  or sore head Mrs Trusells receipt who has sold e water for
50 shillings a pint & 30 e lowest rate

Take stone horse dunge and draw it twice in a limbecke to a quart
of this licqur take a quarter of a pint of e oyle of extracted rosmary
& such a quantity of extracted hony put these into a glase botell & shake
them together for an hower together
ye oyle & rosmary us to be bought at a Chymist a dutchmans in high
holborn neare grayes Inn feild this is to be distilled any tyme in e yeare

+ For a Scald head or to cuer any scabes

Take a Breast of Mutton & rost it & bast it with tar till e
mutton comes to be naught & dryed like a stick take e
moister out of e driping pan & rub it up & down till
it comes all to one body wth this annoynt thinly & let
it dry on & scale off of it self night & morning adding
e quantity of a litle bean of uiue sulpher to an
ounce of this oyntmt, This receipt from Do: Willowby
& much Commended by him

+ ffor a scurfe in e head t whit

boyle tar & fresh butter & tobacka together & a litle linseed oyle cut
e hair clos to e head anoynt it well put on a cap & keep it uery
warm for 3 dayes n take new milk boyle a handfull of Cammomell
in it wash e head warme wth it & anoynt it again & so tye it up 3 days
as before this doe a fortnight together or as long as need requirs
washing still wth e milk & cammamile before you anioynt it & after
you have done anoynting it keep e partys head warm for a fortnight

For the stone an Electary

Take Cassia newly drawn 1/2 an ounce, Chosen Rubarb in
pouder one dram & halfe & halfe Cyprus Turpentine well wash'd
3 drams Species Diatraganthis frigidi a scruple, Liquorish in pouder
halfe a dram, mix these together in a Sufficient quantity of Syrop
of Althea.
Take e quantity of a good walnut in e Morning fasting & drink
a good quanty of plain posset drink wi it an hour after make an
other posset drink wth white wine sweeten it wth Syrrop of Althea
drink likewise a good draught then walk & eat wht yr Appetite invites

This Receipt was sent & Recomended by my Lady Cavendish who writt
May e 24th 1716 t she had done great cures wth it on some of her poore
Neighbours & is so safe & gentile a Medium t she gave it to a child of 4
years old t had e stone from her Cradle to t degree t e child never lay
in a bed: all told her & Parents she cou'd never be curd; but has never
had e least pain since she took it & never a night wthout befor. It is a
Receipt of old Dr Ridgleys t he gave to a perticular ffreind of his
t curd him whn at a great Age. This was sent by my Lady to Mis Hussey
at Bloxham.

For all sorts of paines or Impedimts in e Head

Take Hissop Rosemary violets Gervine Betony Herb Iohn Mouseare Evangi plantane Sage
featherfew of each an handfull & stamp them & then infuse them in a gallon of whit wine
then distill them the next day That water is good for any dropsy Impostune or Megerin
or any other Impedimt or ach in the head whatsoever taking a glass fasting in e morning.

The flower of Salues
Take Rosin & pearosin of each 1/2 li virgins was & frankinsence of each 4 oz Mastick
1 oz hartstallow 4 oz 1 oz of Alebainum Camphire 2 dram Melt that which is to be
Melted & powder that wch is to be powdred boile all these together till they are throwly melted
then strain it throw a Canvas strainer into 2 quart of whitwine boile all together & let it
stand till it be bload warm then put to it a qter of a pound of Terpentine stirring it
continually till it be throw cold, be sure it be no more then bloud warm when you put in
the terpentine & Camphire for if it be it will marr all your stuffe when it is throw cold
oyle your hands & make it into Rolls & keep it as a pure Salue for all kind of wounds
& sores new or old It cures fistulas the Knigs Evill & sinews shrank driis out splinters
of broken bones heals the wound & breeds good flesh

For a numb palsey

Take mustard ceed Pimpernell Crowfoot & a clot of mastick all these well bruised & mixed
with the bloud of a goat & put thereto good Allegeere a little & so let them stand 3 daies
& then put them vnder the Cap of a Stillory & still it This water will help a man of the
stone if he drink thereof euy day fasting the stone shall avoid from him as it were Sand
Itm this water drank fasting wth Castory destroyeth all maner of palsey if it be not dead
in the sinews or members It heals scald heads & makes the hairs grow if washt therwith
If a man be scallded wash him with this water & in 9 dayes he shal be whole
of all other medicines it comforteth the sinews for the palsey.

For the Phrensy & Tissick

Take yong pidgeons make them into pouder & medle them well with
Castory & a little Aysell a little of the powder of Elecampane & Germanders
dried in the shade and infuse them in a quart of black cherry water
then distill the water & this water drank fasting helpeth the frensy & Tissick
within 9 days it will help them Itm this water cureth the falling sickness
if the pson haue not had it long giving it them 3 dayes before the full of
the moon & 3 days after anointing the nostrills & Temples with the spirit
of Castor & Juniper abstaining from all salt meats & things that be of
hard decoction These things with Gods assistance will cure and in a
months time you will find the benefit

for grips x aproved remedy t cureth speedyly

& take of it a quarter of a pint of it 3 or 4 tymes a day they must melt e fat wth it

For all maner of Sores in the Eyes a precious water

Take the Rootes of piony the Crops of red ffennell Egrimoy honysuckle
Salendine Rue Chickweed Pimpernell Philipendula the tendor leaues of a red vine
Smalledge Euphrase sowthistle Red roses strawbery leaves vervin & cinqfoils & sage
and betony of each a like quantity about half a handfull wash them clean &

ounce of the powder of Tully and put thereto a qter of a pint of womens brestmilk
that doth nurse a manchild & about 2 spoonfulls of vrine of a man child about
a yeare old & as much honey & let them stand 3 dayes and then distill it in a Stillory
and keep well this water in a Glass vessell that no ayre come thereto and when you

Mrs Lees Receipt of Flos enguentoru 1699: this is e white wch sheft is best

R of Rozin and parrozin of each half a pound deersuet virgins wax & Frankincene of each
a qter of a pound Mastick 1 ounce Camphire 2 drams pure Turpentine 4 ounces let your Rozin
parrozin and Mastick be beat into fine powder & sifted each by themselues then take the suet & wax
& melt them on a soft fire in a new pipkin keeping them continually stirring till they be melted
then put in the Frankincence & when that is melted put in your powders still stirring them till
all be dissolved then strain them throw a fair Canvas into a pottle of whitwine being made hott
in a clean earthen pipkin: then let it boile together till more than half the wine be consumed
keeping it continually stirring then take it from the fire & let it coole vntill it be but bloud warm
then put in yor Turpentine & your Camphire & keep it continually stirring til it be cold & wilbe
taken vp in a Lump then make it vp into Rolls & keep it for vse it is the most precious salue
that can be made it will hold good 20 years.
The Camphire must be beat into powder before you put it in & you must oyle yor hands
with the best oyle of olive when you make it up els it will stick to them all things in s cost 5sh:
made 1677

The vertues.

This salve is good for all sores & wounds old or new it cleaneth & healeth very well
it ingendreth flesh & healeth more in 7 days then others in a month it suffeth no Corupcon
nor proud flesh to ingender therein It is good for the Head ach or swimming in the Braine
or for Impostums in e head or body and for all maner of aches or sprains It draweth out
any thorne or broken bone out of any wound It is very good for any biting or stinging of any
venemous beast It draweth & healeth all manner of botches & boiles It is very good for a Cancer
or wolse in the breast It draweth all manner of paines out of the Liver veines or Spleene
being spread upon sheeps leather & aplied to ye grieved place It is good to make a seircloth
for the Gout being spread thick upon sheeps leather & not removed in 8 or 10 days it healeth
any pestilentiall botch & is good to be taken in pills for ye spleen or for any inward blede.
this is from manys experience counted all s vertues most true & more:

ffor gripes or Loosness

Take a nutmeg beat it to powder & then take a new laid egge &
put out e white & put e powder of e nutmeg into e shell to
the yelk & so bake e ege in an oven wth bread seting it in ashes t
it may stand & it will come out in a ball wch you may keep as
long as you will & take of it in ale or wt you will as much as will
lye on a 6d.

To stop e Coming down of e tearms by a midwife

if they flow immoderatly take a greasy dish cloth & boyle
it in a pinte of milke & so apply it as hot as she can bear
& as it cooles heat it again & so doe 4 tymes for 3 or 4
mornings together & if they stop let blood 10 or 11 ounces

To bring m down in young maids

let them take Roman wormwood gentian roots Cammamile flowers Cetury
& boyle a good quantity of e dryed herbs in a quart of strong bere or
Ayle & a quart of comon table bear mixed together & drink half
a pint, morning & after none & this doth help them

A powder to take away a wean

Take tow or 3 small bones of a dead body t  e flesh is consumed
in e breaking up of graues you may find m burn thes bones
let m be red hot n wn they be cold beat m to powder &
searse them through a fine peice of tifeny & keep it in a
box & straw of this powder on e ween 2 or 3 tymes a day this
will wast e wean away by degrees you must tye a Cloth on e
place to keep e powder from falling off

Another for e Same

Take e clay of from an house t  is made of mud or

from one in a morning till it be thin enough to spread on e
wean & so keep doeing dayly & it will treat it

A Receipt for e dropsie

a good fire shovell of ashes & put it into 4 quarts of Ayle boyle it till it

Ride about in e aire after you have took your dose & it will Iog down

For a Cough. Dr Fryar Apr. 1669.
Take foure Scruples of juice of Licorice; two Scruples of Aloës-Rosata,
one Scruple of pills of Cynoglosso, wth syrup of maidenhaire; make this a masse
of pills & of a Drachme make six pills; Whereof take one or two every other
night after a slight supper. Take itt a weeke or longer as he cough lasts.
Then
Take Elicampane rootes greene slic't thin, three ounces, three pints of
faire water & a pound of powdred sugar, mingle these together, & stew them gently
from seven in e morning till seven att night untill halfe bee consumed then take
itt off; Take foure or five Spoonfulls a day one or more att a time, & eat
some of e root wth butter & sugar.
Or
Take a pint of e best aqua vitae & mingle wth itt halfe a pound of good
powder'd sugar, in a broad pewter dish, wch sett upon coales till itt begin to
bubble then sett itt on fire wth a burning paper stirring itt continually, wth
a long spoone, as long as you can make itt burne, by setting itt often on
fire; Then adde to itt, foure ounces of damaske rose water, & give itt a boyle
or two; & take itt for vse a spoonefull of itt three or foure times a
day.

This hath done much goof aboue all others

A Gentle purge

Take to 1/2 an oz of Cena halfe a pinte of springe water in=
fusd cover itt close all night warm but not to boyle with
about a spoonefull of bruised Aniseeds or ffennill seeds
mingld with halfe a race of sliced Ginger in e morninge
strayn itt and then putt some 2 or 3 spoonefulls of sirrop
of Roses to drinke itt
Some will in the morninge giue e infusion of boylinge vp
onely by 2 or 3 cimprings t so e strength of the Cena may
come out e more
Allso some will for more strength of e purge infuse e
Cena instead of water with e decoction of halfe a pinte
of e Liquor t hath some handfull of Pruans boyld in itt
excellent for those inclininge to a Consumption
To cutt ffleame when e defluction of a cold is ouer t itt
thickens allso to purge e longs of e sharpnesse of e defluction
itt purgeth the gall which often causeth e Iandise and many
disseases helpes deiestion this taken springe and fall from
Monsieur Lagoard who hath done much good by itt.
