A Souerain Remedy for a sore breast or any
other swelling

Take a pinte of e Iuce of groundsell & mix therwth 6 or 7 or 8
spoonfulls of wheat flower e worse e flower is e more you must put
in & stir m together then take e yelks of 2 or 3 eges & beat m uery
well & put m in n boyle it all together till it be a uery thick
pultice wn you have taken it of e fire put in 3 spoon fulls of raw
hony & stir it together & lay some of it on a cloth as hot to e breast
as e party can sufer it renew ^it euening & morning this will either
dissoule e swelling t it shall not break or if it be broken lay this
pultice to it & it will both draw & heale so you aply nothing else
this hath been much used & hath done rar cuers wth litle pain

The Yellow Salue of my Lady Husseys 29 Sep: 1684

Take 4 ounces of venise terpentine as much Bee wax, almost as much Rozin
a little bitt of fresh butter Boyle all these together in a Skillet & as it boils it will rise vp
to the top of the Skillet & then it will fall of it selfe again. then take it off (it is boild enough)
then power it forth into oyster shells & so keep it or in a gally pott.

A Medecin for a strain to be used at
e first geting it or after

Take a napkin & fold it in 4 doubles more or less according to e greatness of
e strain & dip it in cold watter & lay it to e strain & then heat a wollen
blanket as hot as you can & wrap it in it & so doe for 2 or 3 tymes if e
strain Continue this hath helped m wn straind 2 or 3 weeks

To make a Salue called flos. unguentium

Take of Rosin, perrosin of each one pound of virgins wax Olibanum,
frankencense harts or dear suett & venus terpinten ^(& mastick 2 ounces) of each one pound, powder t wch  will
powder, & cut that wch will not, then melt e rosin, perrosin, mastick
olibanum & deeres suett together in a pan afterward put in your mastik
finly powdred then set them on e fire & keep m well stiring that they seeth not
over, & so let m boyle gently, you stiring m till you thinke m well mingled
together, then strain it as hot as posible you can, thro a strong Canvas bag
into a pan of about 2 galonds t hath in it about 3 pintes ^quarts of whit wine, ready
heated, n set it again on e fire & boyle them halfe a quarter of an hower,
allwaise stiring m then take it from e fire, till e scume be off, then put
to it 2 drames of Camphir finly powdred or sliced, & stir m well a while, then
put in your venus turpintin still stiring it, till it be cold, or so stif that it
will be stired no more, then you may roule it up you must have e help of 2
men to stir it t wn one is weary e other may work & the other hold e pan
a slice must be got about half an eln long like a puding slice
half or a quarter of this quantity you may make at a tyme
they must oyle thir hands t work it & e trencher on wch they work it
if any of e whit wine work out it is good to keep to wash sores
Cap it up in oyle paper

An Excelent Poultice for a sore breast

Take a black sheepes head wth e wooll on it & boyle it in spring
watter about a galond & half till it be all to mash e meat
from e bone on a gentle fire then take half a pound of rice
& boyle to a pultice in e broth then take oyle of cammamile
& oyle of Roses of each an ounce & 2 peny worth of saferon
& the yelk of an egge beaten among them mix all this together
& lay it on e pace place affected luke warm spreading it on a
lingin cloth if e breast be broken in several holes then
take Goat dung sifted & searsed into powder & take a tent
of lint & dip it in e powder & to apply it this allso will
desoulu a Cancor; & is said never to fail to cuer a breast.

Another Pultice to break a sore breast

Take 2 handfulls of Mercury 2 handfulls of Moules prick them
clean & shread them & small take allso a good handfull of Chickenweed
or 2 handfulls, a good handsome peice of swins grease & put them
into milk & boyle them very well then thicken it wth bread crumbs
or wheat bran like a pultice then take it off e fire & put in half
an ounce of fenygrick small beaten every time you apply it to
e breast make e quantity you lay on as hot as e patient can
abide it & mix it wth the yolk of an egg & it will break e breast
then to heal it take red sag & groundsel of each a handfull shread
very smal then take goats grease & beat m very well together
untill they become a pretty smoth salve & so work little of it in
your hand untill it be warme then apply it to e sore & it will heal
it you must shift it night & morning e abovesaid pultice must be
laid above e salve to keep e breast warm

for a milk sore in e breast

Take 2 ounces of bees wax melt it n put to it 4 ounces of
deer sueet or fresh Butter wthout salt if you cannot get
e deer sueet 2 spoonfulls of hony 6 spoonfulls of whit
wine vinagar let it boyle up between every thing you put in
then take it off & put in 2 grated nutmeges 2 spoonfulls of Brandy
brused peper stir it till it is cold, Let e woman keep her breast
clean drawn from e milk lay e pultice all over e breast only
cuting a hole in e Cloth for e nipell, pultice it at e first once
a day if e breast is not brock but if so, take it off every night
& wipe it & lay it on again warm & if this is a humor it will
dispurse it, or if it breaks, it will help it to break & heal it againe

An approved Salve to heal & draw any Impostum
fistilow or sweling in any part of e body

Take smal red docks & pull off e leaves & take e midle stripes
as I say e leaves stript off & take e same striges or stringes &
wrap a handfull or 2 of stripes in e leaves & lay m in e Embers
to be rosted very tenderly if you want leaves to put e striges in
thus to rost in e fire then take paper & to let m rost in e Embers
a 1/4 of an hower untill they be very tender well rosted, then
take m off & put some hogs grease amonge e stripes & so wth
your knife bruse it & mingle them together & it will be as a salve
wth wch take some of it & spread it upon some dock leaves or els
take a cloth & spread it on t & lay it to e place as hot as they can
sufer it & this doe 2 ^tymes on a day for this will both heal & draw all
swellings or Impostums or fistilows this a french man used to a
woman who had a wonderful sore eye t she could take no rest
who aplyed this upon her eye, in this manner first her eye was
closed & a litle leafe of e dock laid along the eye lid & this
medicine was Laid then upon e eye very hot, & this did fetch
out both e pain & draw out e Corruption & heal it, & it will
doe e like upon any sore, & this is a most soverain medicine

To kill an Itching in wounds wn healing or
scabs
Take a pottle of smiths watter a good handfull of red sage & as
much Alum as 2 hens eges & 2 penith of hony & boyle all this
together in an Earthen pot & let it boyle well 1/4 an hower & take
it off e fire & keep it wth e hearbs for your use, & warme a litle
of it in a poringer blood warme & wash e place morning & night
& this will kill any Itching in e flesh or scabs or ^in any part of
e body

A good Poultice for any swelling

A good Poultice t cuers sore breast or hard
swelling making m to breake & cuers wthout salves

Take a pint of milk or wt quantity you like a quarter of a
pint of fine oatmeale a quarter of a pound of good fine
bores grease half a spoonfull of Linseed oyle a quarter of a pound
of hony 2 handfulls of cammamile shred small boyle all thes

if e sore be broke you need put on no salve to it for this pultice

To make a watter t cuers wounds & sores wthout
plaisters

Take in May 3 quarts of new mike whey & let it be milk
measure boyle it till it come to little more n a quart & put to
it a good handfull of oaken leaues & more ^be half ashen leaves e
first puting out t lookes red a handfull of young Elmer
leaves & topes of Briar leaves boyle all thes well together
& some water creasses, boyle m wth a quarter of a pound of
Alum, & wn all is well boyled wring out e liquour from
e hearbs & let it setlell & so put it up for your use & wn you
use it wash e sore well wth it once in 24 howers this watter
will keep 2 years

A pultice to break hard swellings

Take of cheekweed a litle, & silegreen t growes upon walls, & whit
lilly leaves & boyle m in milk & then put white bread to it haveing
shread your herbs small & put in a litle bors grease & to apply
it twice a day till it be broke laid on as warm as they can
bear it & if you se it doe not break in 4 or 5 dayes put in
some whit lilly root to it

192
A very Souerian Oyntment for any Ach or payne Swelling
or bruise or sore breast & likewise to make a Salve ^of for it
for any Sore

To make e Oyntment
Take parsley, Sage Smalidge, Arpin, Cumfry, Southernwood marygold leaves,
Housleek, Valerin, wormwood Selfheal, Biteny, mercury, Scabuous Herbgrase
plantine Ribb, herbe Benit Lyonstooth Buckshorn, Take of every one
of these 4, of the phisike herbwomens handfulls, & pick e dry stalks
from them, wash them very well set them a dreyning two dayes, & when they
have stood e full tyme, then swing them in a Cloth very well, then Chop them
very Small & take 4 pound of fresh Butter out of the Churne in May and
put e Butter & herbs together in a bottel, or Skelit, you must not let your
fire be to hott, but keep them Stiring on the fire, & when they have boyled
a good while, take them of e fire, & take a Streyner, & two Earthen pannes,
& streyn it into one, & as you Streyn it put it into e other, & when you have
done let it be could, & then take of e topp & put it into an Earthen vesill, and
Clarife it on e fire & take what quantity you please for Salve, & keep e rest
for oyntmt, that which you make for Salve take Bees wax, Rosin & venus
the wax somewt more n e rest
turpinten a like quantity of these 3,  being alltogether they must be as much
as halfe e Oyntmt, or better, which you make for Salve wch melt alltogether
& Simber a littel on e fire & pour it into a galley pot, & it will keep 2 or 3
yeares, it should not touch any pewter or Brase, but e keytell that boyled
it, for it puterifyes it, this Salve is very good for old sores, or green sores,
for paynes, aches or swellings the patient that is anoynted wth it must
not anoynt themselves, but some other to anoynt them when they use it they
must anoynt against e fire, and lay a warme Cloth to e place greived
approved by Mary Langham

A Receipt of Alderman Langhams wife of a sere cloth
allmost for any thinge as aches swellings sprains soares,
Bruses, cuts, To draw and heale if not an hot humour

Take 1/2 a pinte of salet oyle, 1/4 lb of red Lead sersed finly through
a sive mix the oyle & lead well together, then set it on e fire, allwaise stiring
of it wth a flat stick while it is in being boyling & when it is allmost boyled
enough e lead will turne blackish And to know when it is enough try it
thus by diping a peice of Cloth in it, wch wth your finger feel, if it stick
not greesy greasy when it is a littel cold if it doe not Cleave to e finger
it is boyled enough, Then take as much as a walnut & Somwhat more
of Bees wax, being cut ready in peices before wch put in as soon as ever you
take e oyle & lead of e fire, stiring it till it be melted then dip your
cloth in the aforesaid Composition haveing a Stick ready oyled to hang it
to coole, while you dipe e rest the hotter you dip e finne Coalesed
94





your cere cloth with be, & if you have much to dyie as the stufe cortes in diping you may heate it againe, but not let it boyle, so soon as you can handle ye CereCloth, before it be neare Cold it must be stretched out between 2 by ye fire yt all places may be alike

For Jackings in the Body Isues a good plaster
Take Beeswax 6 ounces Venus turpentine 3 ounces Cinaber beaten of fine powder & mixt with a llittle Salladoyle half an ounce of Minium 3 ounces & half Orras finely beaten half an ounce of musk 2 grains mixt with a little rosewater Let all these be melted together till they be incorporated & be sure it do not boile then dip yor cloth in it (any thin linen Cloth fit for that purpose then oyle it with Sallad oyle & smooth it with a slikston on a plain board

To cuer ye Itch
First take brimston & milke 3 or 4 mornings together to draw it out then take about half a pound of unsalted butter or wash ye salt out of it very well & put to it a good quantity of flower of Brimston & powder of allum & ginger & some sope & make it into a salve & put some powder of peper into it & put to it to take away ye smell some powder of nutmege or a nutmeg grated & 2 or 3 spoonfulls of brandy & anoint your self well all over wth it befor a good fire for 3 weekes every night & anoint Espectily ye Joynts wher you find it most & wear one shift all ye tyme let it be a bad one yt you use & wear thread stockings becase they may be washed, & let your lining you wore before be well scalded & blecht for they are apt to Infect again, you may if you will warm your bed wn you goe to it this hath cured thos yt have had it 12 mounths, & take after it some purging phisick to Clear ye blood, & at a mounths End take clean sheets & air ye blankets well befor a fire & if winter hang ym allso out in ye frost & warm ye feather bed very well wth Colles

Another
Take roach allum & steep a good quantity in as much watter as will wet your shift & wn it is steeped a night or more then dip your shift & let ye watter dry in wth out much wrig wringing out & wn it is throughly dry weare it a week or more & yn doe another so & weare it so doeing till you find ye itching gone let it be an old shife for it will stain

Another
Take ye watter yt smiths quench their irons in & wet your self all over morning & night befor a fire so doe till you are well

To cuer ye Itch

A Receipt of an Exelent Ballsom good for all sores
inward or outward of Sr Richard Knapers
The La Husseys

Take a new Earthen pipkin that is well glased wch containeth 3 gallons & put there
to 2 quarts of Springe watter 2 quarts of Sallet oyle one  of St Ihonswort Dragons
bloud an  mumea one  sake half a pint liquid Storax 1/2 an lb of Venues turpuntin
half a lb; Cochinall one , then take another pipkin and put therein of yealow bees
wax one lb sweet Marjerome Bay Leaves and time one handfull Rosmary topes one
handfull let these two pipkins stand ouer an indiffrent hott fire of Charcole for the space
of half an hower then powre this Bees wax & hearbs into the other pipkin and Let itt
stand so a little while, then put in of red Sanders beaten to powder one  and stire itt well
till you thinke itt be Coulred and see itt boyle; then take itt ofe the fire and power itt into
a broad Earthen milkepan then put into itt one  of naturall balsome and straine it hard
through a Strainer into another Milke pan; so let it stand till it be cold which you may
know by a Cake that will rise on the top of it the Longer it stands if it be till the next
morning; the thicker will the cake be then cut it and melt the cake in a pewter dish
and power it into pots for you use the best time to make it is the midle of Iune
as allso save the Liqued water under the cake to wash sores befor you aplye the
Ballsome for the v Vertus of this Balsom see the 2? 23 page of this Booke

To make oyl of Swallows

Take Seuenten Swallows from the nest & cut them into peices feathers & guts, take
Cammomile Rue, plantine, Ribwort bay leaves, peny Royall, dill, hysop Rosmary
Sage, St Iohns wort 1 handfull shred them & boyle them in a pinte of Sack, & 4
of Salet oyle untill e herbs be Crispt & the wine wasted, then strein the oyl to
keep it

This is good in all aches palseis Convoulsions which proceed from Cold Causes paine
in the sid Coughs wind in the Belly mothers Chafe some of it into e parts afected
& cover them with a brown paper covered wth wooll.

Against a bruse

Take Salendine soden very soft in running watter, make therof a plaister and
apply it to e sore.

To make oyl of Swalows good for e hearing

Take young swallows strip of thir feathers, & cut them in little
pecies & put m in a pot or Iuge t will goe into a pan wher in is
hot cold watter stope e pot they are in very close, & then let e
watter boyle fast till you thinke they are boyled so as to have e
licqur run from m then keep it for your use & drop some of
it into your ear ^warm goeing into bed & lye on e contrary side that it may
not goe out, this hath cured thos t were very deafe
allso for deafness drop in the ear some of the Iuce of ground
Iuey & stop e ear wth some of e leaves 2 or 3 nights
together or longer

A Receit given vnder hand and Seale by a Southerne
Phisition to the Sherriff of York as a Speciall
gratification for Some favours done to him by
the Sherriff

Take 4 handfulls of Sage beat it in a Stone morter like greene Sauce
then putt in a quart of clarrett wine lett it Soe Stand 3 or 4 daies Sterring it
twice or thrice then lett it Stand and Settle and the next morning take of
the Sage wine 3 Spoonfulls and of runing water one Spoonefull, fast
after it an hower or better, Vse this from michas tell the end of March:
It will cure any ach or humor in the joynts, Dry Rhumes keepe from all deseases
to the 4th degree, It will helpe the Dead palsey and convulsions of the Sinews
It Sharpens the memory and from the beginning of taking of it it will keep
the body milde strengthen nature till the fullness of yor daies be finished
noe thing wilbee changed in yor strength Except the change of yor haire It will
keepe the teeth Sound that they be not corrupted It will keepe you from the gout
and dead palsey the Dropsey or any Swelling in the joynts in the body It will alsoe
helpe women in conseption if they vse it 20 daies together, this by the helpe of Almighty
God hath kept me in this Estate from 50 to 85 yeares old Gods name be Glorifyed

A Receipt of a rare Conuoulsion water Mr Gap
at Sleford

Take of e flowers of e Tily or Linden tree 3 handfulls of flowers
of Lillyes of e ualese five handfulls, of Piony seeds half an ounce
let these be Infused in 5 pints of e best whit wine for e Space of 8
dayes then distill m wth a gentill fire afterwards take of e flowers of
Rosmary half an handfull flowers of Lauender one handfull e leaves of
Rue one handfull Betony half a handfull Stachas flowers of Arabia
one pugill Single piony roots 2 drames & a half ditany of Crest
2 drames & a halfe prepared Squills a drame & a half Pelotory of
Spain half a dram, Misleto of e oak two drames Castorum a dram
Cubabs & Cardemonyes each a scruple mace half a dram cloves
tow scruples nutmeges a scruple Let all thes being rightly prepard
be Infused in e above said distillation for the Space of 6 dayes
longer afterwards destill m as aforesaid
It may doe well to give e Child half a Spoonfull of black Chery
watter or Simple piony water at a tyme euening & morning for 3
dayes together both about e full & Chang of e moon for some mounths
together the same quantityes allso of thes may be given at any other
tyme & as often as need shall requir upon any appearance or fear
of Conuoulsion fitts e waters may be lightly sweetened wth fin Sugar
or Surrup of Piony wch is better
One or 2 mornings together in a mounth t is tow say about a week
before e full of e moon it may doe well to purge e Child wth a spoonfull
or tow of surrup of damask rosses or wth such a quantity therof as
may move 2 or 3 stolles a day It may be given ether a lone or in a
small quantity of bere
Also upon any appearance or fear of such fites it may doe well to
make use of oyle of Amber applying it to e nap of the neck temples
of e head & nostrills 2 or 3 dropes to each part
Probably an Isue in e Left arme might tend much to e
preseruation prevention of such fitts

Take of e moderat Spirit of wine & good Canary sacke

Mr Boyles way
Aply a red hot iron as neare to e place as it can posible be indured

To stay vomiting
Boyle a handfull of e topps of spear mint & a handfull of
dryed red rose buds in a pint of Strong white wine vinagar till
a good part of e vinegar be wasted then straine it & wett
e spunge in it t is pretty thin and as broad as your hand
& apply it to e pitt of e stomach as hott as may be suffered
wetting it againe as often as you see occassion & binding it
fast on wth a swathe. The boyled mint & roses layd plaister
wise to e stomach have e same vertue but they must not
be wrung too dry from the vinegar

To Strengthen e Stomach

Take old marmalet of quinces and old conserve of red Roses,
of each of m e quantity of a pretty big wallnut mix
these well together wth as much metheredate as a nutmege
& spread it upon a A peice of a pretty thick glove leather
cut fit for the pitt of e stomach, when you lay it on
strew some powder of saffron on e metheridate quince
& rosses & wear it till it be quite dry you may then renew
it againe as you find benifit or occassion.
You may add to e other Ingredients e powder of dryed minte
if e party be subiect to vomitings
Thes medicins have been tryed with good successe

if e stomach be foule take a vomit of Cardus posit drink then

Dr Lowers Collick watter

Take 3 quarts of double or single stilled aneseed watter; put to
it Guieum chips sena from Alexandria, alicompane, Coriander seed
prepar'd Licquorice sliced of each two or three ounces, you may add
to it one once of sweet fenill seeds, steep them 12 dayes, then straine it

them 12 dayes howers in vinager , then dry m in e sun or by e fire
you may take from 3 spoonfulls to five at a tyme & if you bring it up you
must repeat it or if you have no cause in 2 or 3 howers you may repeat
it likewise.
if you have a mind to make e medicine strong you may put 3 ounces
of e Ingredients instead of two

+ An approved medeccin for e gout

New milk warm from e cow take & bath your feet or hands wch
part is troubled wth e gout & so continue m  in e milk about 2
howers doe this 3 or 4 tymes aday, e same milke will serve for
one whole day & so doe e next day or longer as you find your pains
you are not to warme it on a fire but as it came at first from e
cow for your feet will warme it in e useing. This will draw avay
e pains & streingthen e part

10 e rind wth e pulp sliced of nutmeges 1 ounce let all thes stand infused
& massarated 3 dayes in 8 pintes of e best white wine then distill it in a
cold still take of this euery morning a little wine glasse or more as you se
occation

Do Fryers Receipt for e Scuruie

Take sea Scurui grasse broklime & water cresses of each on pound halfe
a pound of e roots of sasafarx & 3d of saferon to be tuned in 3 galonds of
new bear or ale & drinke a glasse full euery morning Spring & fall

for e Scuruie or mangie

The bran of wheat being dryed brown but not burnt & e powder therof
mixed wth whit wine boyled in sharp Vinagar & rubed upon m t be scuruie
or mangie easeth e party uery much

for e Scurvi
Take Iuce of oringes Iuce of scuruigrase & Iuice
of Broklime & watter cressys & let m stand a litle
to setle & of this take a small glasse full a fort-
night together fasting

for e Scurui Mrs Dones Receipt

Take dandelion & boyle it in milke drinke of this morning & at night
& it will help m & has cured thos who have been brought very low
107





221
for spiting of blood

Past made of fine meale such as bookbinders use helpeth all such
as doe spit blood being taken warm at once

for spiting by ruhme

Take e best hony you can get & eat of it in e morning or any
tyme of e day half a spoonfull at a tyme about 2 ounces in
a day or more if you will in e morning eat wth bread tosted or
as you please

222
ffor wattering Eyes
The graines of wheat being dryed brown but not burnt & e powder
ther of mixed wth whit wine is good for wattering Eyes if it be laid
theron or therto

for eyes t have a speke in them it takes
it away in a litle tyme
Take a peice of new holand or douglas ^or Cambrick & wrape it up as a
peice of paper, & burn it on a pewter plat & take a drop of
e oyle of it & put it into e eye morning & night & you will
find it lessen e speck in t growes in e sight & tak it
away they t told it said e receipt was worth 100li

To draw a ruhme from e eyes

Take a bullet t has been shot through a gun & beat it very thin &
make holles in it & bind it to e nape of e neck & it will draw e rume
away

ffor sore eyes or red eyes wth ruhme

Take 2 or 3 spoonfulls of good clear uarges & put to it of
Lapis caleminarys & tutte of each a penith made into
fine powder & put thes in a litle botell corked & shake it
wn you use it & wet your eyes wth it goeing to bed on the out
side of the eyes or ofener in a day this will keep half a year

for eyes t have skins or mists before m used &
Commended by do Thurberwell an Oculist

Take of white rose watter & Nants Brandy t wch is right & comes from thence &
mixe an equall quantity of each together & so wash your eyes wth it 2 or 3
tymes a day & it will clear your sight

an Aproved medicien for e eyes

Take a little Lapis Caleminaris a quarter or half an ounce
& put it into halfe a quarter of good Canary, shake it & let it
stand while e powder is setled then take a few dropes in a Spoon
wn tis clear & wth your finger dab it upon your eye lides & then
twincle it into your eyes then shake your botle & let it settle
before you use it againe
one in year said he saw better since he had used it n he had done in 10 years before

An Extreordinary smoak for e eyes

Take Lignum aloes, storax sasafras barke, amber, of each one drame,
cut bettony half an ounce Rosmary flowers or Rosmary half a quarter
of an ounce & as much Tobaca as Rosmary mixe wth it take this in
a pipe when you are goeing to bed or as often as you please
108





223
for e Kings Euell
Take barley meale tar wax oyle & e urin of a young
boy & it doth degest hard swellings in e throat called e Kings Euell

A dyit drinke to purgee blood
to cuer  Kings Eveill e Lady St Barbars receipt
Take Monk Rubarb roots, four ounces, oswin roots,
6 ounces (wch is watter vern or veruin) 6 ounces, east
 harran harrow harrow roots 3 ounces: Reasans
stoned of e Sun, 3 pound: boyle thes in five galonds
of Ayle worte, till one galond be boyled away, then
strain it & wn cold, worke it wth yeast as you doe Ayle,
& tun it up: & when it has done working, put in e bag
of hearbs mentioned on e side as be for directed.
The bag of hearbs wild Angelico; Sanicle: Mugwort; wood betony: Scabious: Dandelion: White basle leavs: Arck angel flowers: Ribwort: Buglos: Plantain: Comfry: Avans: worm wood t grows in a march: violet leaves: Whit wild dragons: Take of thes leaves a small hand full cut m & put m into a bag wth a waight in it tyed at top: a fasten the string to e bung; t e bag may hang in e midle of he uesill of drinkee bag of  herbs is to be put into e uesill of
drinke wn it has done working
This drinke must be e Constant drinke for e tyme it
lasts, & must be drunke new & if in danger of
growing stale while a drinking, bottle it up, all off
when towards growing stale.
This dyet drinke has done good to persons in an ill
habit of body, and to Children suspected to be subiect
to sharp humours & runing, as he Kings Euill &
fistulows or e like
And a child at had a breaking out twise t was
thought by e Surgens to be a fistowlow humour:
drinking this drinke Spring & fall for a year or
tow never had any such swelling again: it had an
issue made in its arme
The roots are to be had at e Apothecarys & many of
e hearbs are to be taken in thir season & dryed for
use, for they last but a litle tyme green

224
good agt Poyson
The roots of Spyderwort tuned up into new ale & drunk for a mounth
together Expelleth poyson allthough it have uneu?selly spread
it self throug out e body

To cuer a scald or burn
When it is just burnt let e first thinge you doe to it be to beat 2 or 3
whits of eges & 2 or 3 spoonfulls of e best salit oyle & beat m well to-
gether till it be pretty thick & then anoynt e burn all over wth it with
a feather & so keep it wth this to be wet till you can get e folowing
pultice made for it wch must be thus take a pint of milke or more if
it be a larg burn & take a litle handfull of housleeke & as much ^green Elder
leaues & some of e Inward rind of Elder & of muline & chickwood and
ground Iuey or ale hoof as much of each of thes as of e other then
shred m & bruse m smal & boyle m wth e milke & smal oatmel keeping
them continually stiring puting in 2 or 3 oynions pounded smal then drese
it again wthe oyl & whit of ege & aply this pultice to it as warm as
they can bear it & so renew e pultice fresh twice a day, it must be
aplyed so long till e humor be turned to a thick corruption & then
anoynt it still wth e whit of ege & oyle, it will probably need e
pultice but 3 or 4 dayes then use e salue called Basilicon wch may
be had at e Apothecarys & spread a plaister t is sig singed the cloth
of it, all over biger then e sore & so keep e one plaister only
spreading a litle new after you have wiped all e Corruption off
from e plaister, & so keep this plaister to it till it is quit whol, at
first you may lay ^on e e plaister on once a day, after once till 2 dayes
may serve, this pultice & salve has done great cuers by Mrs
Pocoke you must rem to put in some mutton suett into e pultice
or mutton dripine

A whit oyntment Excelent for scalds or burns
Take 2 peny worth of Litharge of gold, half a pinte of e best
oyle olive: half a pinte of e best whit wine vinagar keep e oyle & e
vinagar apart, & put e letharge of gold first, reduced to a very fin powder
into a bellmetle skilitt, or such a thinge but it must never come near
a fire stir it wth a silver spoon, first put a good spoonfull of oyle to e
letharge of gold & stir it half a quarter of an hower then ade as much
vinagar: & stir ite tyme as before, so doe again successively e oyle
& vinegar till all is stirred together it will be about 6 howers making stir it
all one way at first it is aboute substance of cream, but grows stifer after
this fetches out e fire immediatly & cuers wthout a scar good for all sores
Exelent for any swellings cancurous humours, & fistolows, it searches to e
botom & then heales but change e lining seldome if put to an old sore,
I have heard it comended to revent scars of e small pox wn they begin to dry
& are all come out
109





223
To stop flooding in Childbed
Take e inward flanill of a mans breaches & put it upon a plate
& set it over a Chafendish of coales wth uinigar in e plate &
let it be well diped & soaked & applyed to e body as hot as owne
can

To cuer the green Sicknesse
Take a quarter of a pound of Treacle an ounce of e fillings
of steele half an ounce of turmerick let it be new powdered
very smalle & stir it very well together euery tyme you take
it wch must be once a day at e first & after a fortnight
twice a day this must be contnued a mounth e quantity of
a nutmege at a tyme at a fortnghts end purge wth surrup
of Bucksthorn & at e mounths end purge againe

224
Good for bruses & aches
Take neats foot oyl & brandy & mix them together & uery
well rub it in befor e fire, & this hath helpt them
t haue been bowed together
Allso oyl of marshmalows mixt wth spirit of wine & well
rubed in morning & Euening is uery good for straines &
bruses

Another
Take oyle of wormes & aquiuita & mix it together & first
rub e place well wth a warm dry cloth & then chafe e
oyle in

Another
Take your own watter & heat, & old peice of Iron hot &
quench it in your own watter & so bath e part with it
allso whit wine uinagar hot & rubed in to e part
streingthens it

To Skin a place new brused
Take brandy & wash it & aply a paper dipt in e brandy & it
will dry it up into a scab if e first doe not doe it dip a 2d
paper

Another to skin a sore
anoint it Unguentum allbum once or twice

Good for an Inward bruse
Take 2 ounces of conserve of red roses & one ounce of Lucatelles balsom
balsom & mix it together & take as much as a nutmege morning & night
e first & last thinge you take & drinke of this ale poset drinke folowing
about a quarter or more of a pinte as warme as you can, make ale poset
drinke about a quart & boyle it 2 or 3 roots of maloues well scraped and
sliced half an ounce of licoras & half a handfull of moringwort let e
thinges lye in your poset drinke till you have taken all this quantity
& then sweeten your poset drinke wth some common, treacle you may continue
this till you are mended this has helped thos t have spit blood upon a
bruse

To Skine a new sore
take bore grease t has not had any saltin it & mix it wth
chalke finly scraped to be like a salve & t will skine & dry
it in 2 or 3 tymes or more as you se fit

for a bite of a uiper or adder
Take live pigions & pull of e feathers near e uent hole
& aply  uent hole to e place bitten & as t pigon dyes
lay on another till they live but take care you doe not
smother e pigeon in holding it
110





225
An Excelent powder to make one sweat & take away
pains & good for Agues, & e Bloody flux
Take Cardus pimpernell Tormentill Betony Scabaus dragon Angelico
Scordium of each one handfull gathered in fair weather not washed
but wiped wth a Cloth brush bruse m & steep them 24 howers in 3
pints of white wine then take one pound of Bolearmenak beaten &
sersed as fine as powder or flower then strain in as much wine from
from e herbs as will moiesten it to be thick & smooth & as it dryes
strain in more wine & stir it often as it dryes in e Sun then strain
e herbs hard t ther be no wine left in them, & wn all t is in & it is a
thick dough take half an ounce of saferon dryed to powder uery fine
& 4 ounces of metheradate mix alltogether & make it up in little balles
& let m dry in e Sun & keep m close & dry for use, wn you use them
scrap it & take it in a glase of warme salt sack or burnt wine or warme
ale & lye to sweat, you may take as much as will lye upon a shilling

225
A Snaile watter good for a Consumption
Take 3 galonds of snailes put m in a paile of watter & a good
quantity of Isope & finill to purge m & a handfull of salt, & wn
they have purged as much as they will take m out of e watter &
stamp m shells & all to peices n put to m 3 pints if stropings of
red Cowes milke of nutmeg Cloves & ginger e weight of a shilling
& of mace 2 shillings of raisons of e Sun stoned half a pound of
blue figes e best t can be got & blew curands shred smal of each
half a pound of yarow 2 good handfulls hartstoung liverwort
sparagras Scabious Egremony hysop & speir mint one great hand-
full beat e Spices & shread e herbs very small mingle alltogether &
distill m in a Cold still close leaded let e watter drop on whit
Sugar Candy
You must take a large wine glass full of this watter at a tyme in
e morning & at 4 in the after noon & an hower before bed tyme

227
for sore niples

Take a pint of thick cream, put into it a handful of yarow
boyl it till it Come to oyle, then strain it & anoynt e niples

228
To kill Rats
2 penyworth of white Arsnick beat it very fine take equall
quantity of wheat flower equall quantity of whit sugar dry them
very well & mix them together & lay so much upon a card made up
like a driping pan as covers e piont of a knife
it kills them by thir snufing it & sucking to it

A plaister for a Quinsey
Take figes 4 ounces, Album greekium half an ounce,
flower of brimston long peper of each a dram, brandy
2 ounces Chymicall oyl of wormwood 16 dropes diacodium as
much as will serve ^or one ounce pigens dunge 2 ounces, beat all these in
a morter till well mixt spread it upon soft leather & lay it
to the throat from ear to ear, & renew it as often as it dryeth
if you have not the oyl of wormwood somme of the hearb dry or
green may serve
